Feature Modules

Feature Modules are optional. The GM switches on the ones they want and tells the players before character creation. Each module below is self-contained.

Module Adds
Magic Alignment, enchanting, spell crafting and the spell lists
Summoning Familiars and undead
Arts and Forms Sword Forms, Martial Arts and Performer Forms
Creatures of the Night Vampires, werewolves and the Returned

Feature Module: Magic

When you choose magic you start with 100 magic points and get 2 spells at level 1, unless you choose a weapon in place of a spell. Spells can be swapped for weapons, and every level after that you can choose 1 magic power or a trait.

  • Magic points go up by 50 every level.

  • Magic DS = 10 + your Senses or Presence rank.

  • Magic Attack ability = Senses or Presence dice.

  • When you cast a spell it is cast as a Main action using any of its levels and costs the amount stated at that level.

In this module: Alignment · Enchanting · Spell crafting · Spell list · Universal Spells · Angelic Spells · Demonic Spells · Structures

Alignment

All magic powers are categorised as Angelic (Senses), Demonic (Presence) or Universal. The alignment meter has seven positions: three Angelic, a neutral centre and three Demonic.

Angelic Angelic Angelic Neutral Demonic Demonic Demonic
3 2 1 0 1 2 3

  • A character with 2 points on either side of the meter cannot use powers of the opposing side.

  • Any Universal power can be used by any alignment.

  • Reaching the maximum on either side gives +5 to all rolls with powers of that alignment and +2 to the Magic DS.

Enchanting

Items are enchanted using spells and magical crystals called Arc Crystals, which hold magical power that is Angelic (blue), Demonic (red) or Universal (white). To enchant an item, you spend the magic points for the spell as an action and use the Arc Crystal.

When enchanting, you roll your magic ability dice, and the result sets the level of enchantment. You must choose between damage or utility. Alternatively you may use both, but only with the lower level’s stats, and you must still make the same DS. Arc Crystals charge the enchant and last 1 combat.

Level DS Damage Utility Arc charges
Novice 8 2 1 4
Adept 9 3 2 3
Master 11 4 3 2

Spell crafting

Spells are crafted using your magic points and 24 hours of uninterrupted concentration for Master, and 10 hours less for every level lower.

When making a spell, you must make a magic ability roll (Senses or Presence) against the DS. The result sets the level of the spell. You must choose between damage or utility. Alternatively you may use both together, but only with the lower level’s stats, and you must still make the same DS roll. For example, an Adept spell can have 5 damage and 1 utility instead of just 8 damage or just 2 utility. This can also be done for range and for combining utility or damage into more potent effects, but should be discussed with the GM. Look at existing spells for inspiration.

Spells can be Universal, Angelic or Demonic. Decide with the GM.

Level DS Damage Utility Range Magic point cost
Novice 8 5 1 1 zone 0
Adept 9 8 2 2 zones 20
Master 11 12 3 3 zones 50

Spell list

All 36 spells below are taken from the Ability Database, which holds the current balanced versions. If a spell here ever differs from the Ability Database, the Ability Database wins.

Universal Spells

Battle Meditation

Range: Self or Touch · Cost: 100 · Duration: 1 Minute / 3 Turns · Action: Opportunity Action

You change the outcome of a roll, making it a Critical Success.

Beastshift

Range: Self · Cost: 50 · Duration: 1 Minute / 3 Turns

You take the form of a beast you have previously seen. You gain all of the beast’s physical attributes but keep your mental ones.

The stats you gain are up to the GM to create.

Blast

Range: 2 Zones · Cost: 10 · Damage Scaling: +6 at 5th level

You fire a beam of energy from your hand. On a hit, deal 6 damage.

Control Animal

Range: 1 Zone · Cost: 80 · Duration: 1 Minute / 3 Turns

You attempt to charm a beast. It must make a Smarts Roll.

On a fail, it is charmed by you for the duration. On a success, the beast knows you tried to charm it.

If in combat, the creature always succeeds on the roll.

Create Structure

Range: 1 Zone · Cost: See table · Duration: 1 Minute / 3 Turns

You create a magical structure that is weak, but will stay up for the duration unless disturbed.

Crush Construct

Range: 2 Zones · Cost: 5 + 5 every level · Damage Scaling: +4 every level

You attempt to crush the parts of a construct, dealing 4 damage.

Enhance Brawn

Range: Self · Cost: Brawn Rank x 10, every turn

+5 to all Brawn Rolls, including attacks that use Brawn, for the duration.

Enhance Health

Range: Self · Cost: 50, every turn

+5 to all Health Rolls for the duration.

Your HP is also increased to x2.

Enhance Speed

Range: Self · Cost: 50, every turn

+5 to all Agility Rolls, including attacks that use Agility, for the duration.

Your movement is also increased by +1 Zone.

Jump

Range: Self or Touch · Cost: 50 · Duration: 1 Minute / 3 Turns

Your jump height is increased by your character’s height for the duration.

Leap

Range: Self or Touch · Cost: 50 · Duration: 1 Minute / 3 Turns

Your jump distance is increased to half a zone for the duration.

Manifest Object

Range: 2 Zones · Cost: 20 · Duration: 1 Minute / 3 Turns

You create an object using magic. The object has the properties of the form it takes (e.g. a key unlocks things).

It lasts for the duration or disperses after use.

Orb

Range: 2 Zones · Cost: 10 · Damage Scaling: +5 at level 4

You gather objects into an orb using magic and can use it as a projectile. On a hit, deal 5 damage.

Shapeshift

Cost: 20 (+40 at level 3) · Duration: 1 Minute / 3 Turns

You take the form of a humanoid you have previously seen.

To see through the new form and know it is a shapeshifter, a creature must make a Senses Roll.

At level 3, you can also replicate voices.

Stealth

Range: Self or Touch · Cost: 20 + 20 every level · Duration: 1 Minute / 3 Turns

You are hidden. Any creature that wants to detect you must make a Senses Roll at Disadvantage.

Duration increases by 1 minute/turn every level.

Stun

Range: 2 Zones · Cost: 50 + 50 every level · Duration: 1 Minute / 3 Turns

You attempt to stun a creature you can see. It must make a Health Roll.

On a fail, it is paralysed for the duration. On a pass, there is no effect.

The number of targets increases by 1 every level.

Summon

Range: 1 Zone · Cost: See tables

You meditate for 1 hour, manifesting a creature you control until it dies.

The creature has the stats of whatever familiar or undead you choose, depending on whether you use Angelic or Demonic magic for the spell.

Telekinesis

Range: 2 Zones · Cost: 10 · Damage: 5

You use magic to move an object or creature. An object cannot resist; a creature that resists makes a Brawn Roll.

On a fail, it is moved in any direction. If it collides with anything, all involved take damage.

Angelic Spells

Absorb Magic

Range: Self · Action: Opportunity Action · Cost: 15 + 10 every 3 levels

You absorb 5 of the damage from an attack that hits you.

The damage absorbed increases by 5 every 3 levels.

Heal

Range: Self or by Touch

You heal by an amount from your healing pool, which equals your Senses Rank x2. This increases to x2 at level 3 and x3 at level 6.

Once your pool is depleted, you must take a light rest to replenish it.

Heal Wound

Range: Self or by Touch · Action: Full Action

You heal 1 wound.

The wounds healed increases by 1 every 3 levels.

Improved Heal

Range: 1 Zone or Self · Cost: None · Prerequisite: Heal Spell

The range of your Heal spell increases to include up to 2 allies within the same zone as you.

Your Heal spell also now cures poisons and venom.

Intangibility

Range: Self · Cost: 20 + 20 every level · Duration: 1 Minute / 3 Turns

You become incorporeal. While in this state, you can neither deal nor take damage, and cannot touch or be touched by anything corporeal for the duration.

The number of turns in combat increases by 1 every level.

Mind Trick

Range: 1 Zone · Cost: 5 + 5 every level

You attempt to charm a humanoid. They must make a Smarts Roll.

On a fail, they are charmed by you for the duration. On a success, the humanoid knows you tried to charm it.

If in combat, the creature gains +10 to their roll.

Projection

Range: 2 Zones · Cost: 20 + 20 every level

You create an illusory image or sound that acts as a distraction for the duration.

To see through the illusion, a creature must make a Smarts Roll.

The number of illusions you can create increases by 1 every 3 levels.

Protection Sphere

Range: Self or None · Cost: 100 + 50 every 2 levels · Duration: 1 Minute

You create a magical bubble around a number of creatures equal to your Senses Rank. It blocks all projectiles and heals everyone inside it by 2 every turn.

While the bubble is active, the caster cannot move.

Demonic Spells

Blood Spear

Range: 2 Zones, in a straight line · Cost: None

You construct a spear of blood and use it as a projectile. On a hit, deal 4 damage.

Choke

Range: 1 Zone · Cost: 50 + 35 every level · Damage Scaling: +4 every 3 levels

You attempt to crush the windpipe of a creature you can see. It must make a Health Roll, taking 12 damage on a fail and half on a pass.

Drain Life

Range: 2 Zones · Cost: 25 + 10 every level · Damage/Heal Scaling: +2 every level

You attempt to drain the life of a creature you can see. On a hit, you deal damage equal to the enemy’s Level and heal yourself by half that amount (round up).

Fireball

Range: 2 Zones · Cost: 50 + 35 every 3 levels · Damage Scaling: +4 every 3 levels

You hurl a ball of fire at a point you can see within range. The creature must make an Agility Roll, taking 12 damage on a fail and half on a pass.

Lightning

Range: 2 Zones, in a straight line · Cost: 50 + 50 every level · Damage Scaling: +3 every 3 levels

A sheet of lightning streaks from your fingers. Each creature in its path must make an Agility Roll, taking 9 lightning damage on a fail and half on a pass.

Poison Seed

Range: Touch · Cost: 200 · Duration: 24-hour ritual

You pour magical poison into a seed over the course of a day, until you have a poison seed.

When the seed is placed into a creature’s body, fungus grows over their skin and they must make a Health Roll. On a fail, they take 12 poison damage each turn until they pass. A pass cures the poison.

Probe Mind

Range: 2 Zones · Cost: 50 + 50 every level · Secrets Scaling: +1 every level

You attempt to extract information from a creature’s mind. It must make a Smarts Roll, or you learn 1 secret the creature knows.

Screech

Range: 2 Zones · Cost: 35 + 35 every 3 levels · Damage Scaling: +5 every 3 levels

You screech in a zone. Every creature in range makes a Health Roll – on a pass they take half damage, on a fail they take 5 damage and are deafened.

Seer Sight

Range: 2 Zones · Cost: 50

You see through the eyes of a creature in range.

After the spell ends, the creature makes a Senses Roll to see if they know the spell was cast on them.

Shadow Sphere

Range: 1 Zone · Cost: 20

You create a sphere of shadows centred on yourself that only you can see, unless a creature has night vision or a light-producing spell.

Otherwise, each creature in range is blinded while in the sphere.

Structures

Used by the Create Structure spell. The cost is paid in magic points.

Type HP CD Weight tolerance Power cost
Small 5 14 30 stone 50
Medium 25 8 50 stone 100
Large 50 4 80 stone 250
Huge 100 2 100 stone 300
Giant 150 0 130 stone 500

Feature Module: Summoning

All summons can be upgraded with new weapons and armour as the player sees fit. Once a new weapon is given to a summon, the player level bonus is lost. If armour is added, its penalty also applies.

A summon may be revived by sacrificing another summon the player controls, and has its stats doubled when brought back to life.

To summon undead, a player needs a corpse, a pile of bones or a gravestone to reanimate. To summon a familiar, a player needs a part from the animal, for example a bear claw for the bear.

In this module: Familiars · Undead

Familiars

Stat Hound Bear Bird
HP 5 + player level 12 + player level 2 + player level
CD 10 + player level 5 + player level 13 + player level
Armour 0 4 0
Speed 2 zones 2 zones 3 zones
Roll to hit Player Presence Player Presence Player Presence
Damage 4 + player level 6 + player level 2 + player level
Scout dice 1d8 1d6 1d12
Magic point cost 60 80 40

Undead

Stat Skeleton Zombie Ghost
HP 5 + player level 12 + player level 2 + player level
CD 10 + player level 5 + player level 13 + player level
Armour 0 4 0
Speed 2 zones 2 zones 3 zones
Roll to hit Player Presence Player Presence Player Presence
Damage 4 + player level 6 + player level 2 + player level
Death Rolls 2 4 1
Magic point cost 60 80 40

Feature Module: Arts and Forms

Combat in After World is enriched by selectable Arts (martial arts stances) and Forms (weapon-specific techniques). Each is activated as an Interaction and lasts until you switch to another. Players can swap forms between encounters, but only one may be active at a time unless a performer combines two at the cost of extra actions.

In this module: Sword Forms · Martial Arts · Performer Forms

Sword Forms

When wielding a sword you can use forms that enhance your skill. Only one form may be active at any given time and cannot be picked with magic unless specified. This is the same for Martial Arts and Performer Forms.

Form Description
Form 1 When used against other swords, gain Advantage on attacks
Form 2 When you are targeted with an attack, add +1 to Combat Defence. If the attack misses, you gain an Opportunity Action against them with your sword only
Form 3 You naturally get critical damage on a hit, but every 2 rounds you suffer 1 Fatigue point
Form 4 (enchanted sword only) Ranged attacks have Disadvantage against you, and if they hit you can attempt to deflect the shot back if you roll higher than the enemy did to hit you
Form 5 When an attack misses you, you can make a non-damaging attack that Stuns the enemy for the rest of that round
Form 6 When attacked with demonic magic, your next hit is a critical, but if you deal over 15 damage it adds 2 demonic points to your alignment
Form 7 When attacked with magic powers you take the damage, but the magic points used for the attack are added to your own. If no points were used you gain 10

Martial Arts

Like Sword Forms, Martial Arts styles can be chosen in place of a trait and last only as long as they are selected, unless specified. Stance DS is 12 + Brawn or Agility rank.

Style Prerequisite Description
Aikido Unarmed When using this stance you can use an Opportunity Action to grapple and flip to the ground any target directly in front of you who missed a close-distance attack against you, dealing 2 damage and making them Grounded
Aikijujitsu Unarmed When you hit with an unarmed attack, as an Interaction you may force the target to make a Health roll against your stance DS. On a fail the target is Stunned for one round
Boxing Unarmed You may attack twice as an Interaction with unarmed attacks, but the second attack deals +1 damage
Arnis 1-handed weapons When you make an unarmed attack while holding one of the listed weapons, add +2 to Combat Defence until the start of your next turn
Iaijutsu Swords or daggers While wielding these weapons you cannot be targeted by Opportunity Actions. You may also use your Interaction to sheathe your weapon, giving your next attack +2 to hit and damage
Kyūdō Bow You may fire your bow as an Interaction
Tendō-ryū Polearms You gain +1 Combat Defence when wielding a polearm. When you hit with an attack you may also sweep the leg and force the target to make an Agility roll against your stance DS. On a fail the target is Grounded

Performer Forms

You may sacrifice your Main action and your Interaction to use two forms at once. This must be repeated every turn or 1 form of the player’s choice is dropped. All forms may be used for yourself or for a number of allies up to your Presence rank -1.

Form Description
Form 1 You have Advantage on all non-combat rolls
Form 2 When you are targeted with an attack and it misses you, you get a 2nd turn for that round (once per round)
Form 3 You naturally get critical damage on a hit, but every 2 rounds you suffer 1 Fatigue point
Form 4 Ranged attacks have Disadvantage against you, and if they hit you can attempt to dodge it by rolling higher than the enemy on an Agility roll
Form 5 When an attack misses you, your attack rolls have Advantage on your next roll
Form 6 You may add an additional Skill die to all rolls once. This lasts until used again or the end of a round in combat

Feature Module: Creatures of the Night

This Feature Module adds creatures of the night to a Fantasy game: vampires, werewolves and the Returned. It uses the rules of the Core System and the Fantasy Theme.

In this module: How lineages work · Using This Module in Other Themes · Vampires · Werewolves · The Returned · Death and Survival · Traits · Balance Notes

How lineages work

Each section gives a set of Basics that every creature of that kind shares, then a list of Covens, Packs or types. A lineage works like a race. Pick one of each kind for your character, and take its stats, languages and size in place of a standard race.

You may only select one Coven, Pack or type as a player, and you must stick to its strengths and weaknesses. If the GM allows it, mixing and matching strengths and weaknesses is possible, but the game has not been balanced for this and it will likely need tweaking.

Lineages are tuned to the same power level as the species in the Star Wars Theme. A strong perk is paid for with a weakness, and a limit such as once per day keeps it in line.

Using This Module in Other Themes

The rules in this module are not specific to any Theme and may be used in either a Fantasy or Modern setting. GMs may still want to tweak certain rules or create custom additions to this ruleset.

Vampires

This section covers the different types of vampire a player can become during the game. GMs can change these as they see fit. The Covens are not made to fit a specific theme and can be used in any.

Vampire Basics

Every vampire gets the following, unless their Coven says otherwise.

Stats

  • Rank 1 in Brawn, Agility or Health ability (choose one)

  • Claws and Fangs: natural Light Melee weapons that scale with you (see below)

  • Supernatural senses: Advantage on Senses rolls to hear and see

  • Undead blood: immune to poison and disease, and Advantage on rolls to resist curses

  • Dead flesh: resistant to weapons that are not silver

  • Thirst for blood (see Vampire Thirst)

  • You do not age

  • You do not need to eat or drink human food and drink. You must sleep, but only for 4 hours

Claws and Fangs

A bite is Piercing and a claw is Slashing. You roll Brawn to bite and Brawn or Agility to claw. Damage is set by the rank of the ability you roll, and goes up by 1 every 3 levels (at levels 3, 6 and 9). Claws and fangs count as one weapon for the Light tag, so you cannot make two natural attacks in one turn unless you also hold another Light weapon.

Ability rank 1 2 3 4
Bite damage 4 5 6 7
Claw damage 3 4 5 6

Weaknesses

Weakness Level 1 Level 2 Level 3+
Sunlight 10 damage per turn 10 damage per turn 10 damage per turn
Silver Ignores Armour Advantage on hits Critical hits
Garlic Poisoned effect Poisoned effect Poisoned effect
Uninvited property 5 damage per turn 5 damage per turn 5 damage per turn
Holy items Frightened effect Frightened effect Frightened effect
Stake Paralysed effect Paralysed effect Paralysed effect

Vampire Thirst

Vampires must feed. They will die without blood, and the rules below show the benefits of feeding. If a vampire goes below 1 point of Blood, they will die after a full 24 hours.

Vampire-exclusive traits and any spells run on Blood Points (BP) instead of Magic. Blood Points are earned differently from Magic, which keeps feeding a meaningful, in-fiction resource rather than a numbers-only cost. An example is Control Animal (Vampire) in the Ability Database, which costs 3 Blood Points (+3 every 3 levels).

  1. Successfully feeding on a living creature grants Blood Points equal to that creature’s Health Rank (their Health attribute score, 1 to 4, which is the same attribute that determines their HP, not their Level or tier), up to the current cap.

  2. Feeding on a creature with no meaningfully defined Health Rank (an ordinary animal, an untrained civilian, and so on) grants 1 Blood Point.

  3. The Blood Point cap equals your Presence Rank x2.

  4. Blood Points do not refill on a rest and are not granted by levelling up. Feeding is the only source. A vampire who has not fed recently can run dry the same way a spellcaster can run out of Magic, but a light rest will not fix it.

House Dracul

Stats

  • Start with Blueblood trait

  • Start with Vampiric Presence trait

  • Start with Vampire Basics

  • Languages: Basic, Vampiric

  • Size: Medium

Known simply as The Old Ones, House Dracul stands above all other covens. Their number has remained fixed at five for longer than any vampire can remember, and unlike other covens they do not create descendants. Beautiful, charismatic and impossibly ancient, they move amongst humanity’s most powerful circles unseen, guiding events from the shadows and ensuring the continued secrecy of vampire society.

The origins of House Dracul are unknown. Some claim they were the first vampires, while others believe they existed long before the curse itself. Whatever the truth, all covens recognise their authority. The Old Ones rarely involve themselves in the affairs of lesser vampires, but when they do, their judgement is final and their will unquestioned.

Vorago Coven

Stats

  • Start with Abyssal Hunger trait

  • Start with 1 extra vampire trait

  • Start with Vampire Basics

  • Languages: Basic, Vampiric

  • Size: Medium

The Vorago Coven, they are vampires whose existence is defined by dependency rather than restraint. Their condition is often compared to the oldest forms of addiction, where blood does not simply sustain them but rewires them. To outsiders they appear unstable, even broken, yet within their own ranks this dependency is understood as the source of their greatest strength. Every act of feeding deepens the bond between power and compulsion, making control an increasingly fragile achievement.

Vorago pass through distinct states of being: Starving, Stable, Empowered, and Frenzied. At their weakest they are brittle, but a single feeding can elevate them into strength far beyond other vampires. Continued consumption pushes them into a dangerous clarity where instinct and hunger begin to merge, until finally reason collapses. In Frenzy they become pure hunters, feared even by their own. Within the Coven, survival is measured by how long one can remain themselves while feeding.

Lethra Coven

Stats

  • Start with Dream Feeder trait

  • Start with Mind Wipe trait

  • Start with Vampire Basics

  • Languages: Basic, Vampiric

  • Size: Medium

The Lethra Coven are defined by absence rather than hunger. They drift into the minds of the living, feeding upon dreams, memories, regrets, fears, and the soft edges of nostalgia. Their presence is rarely recognised; instead, they slip into sleep and thought, slowly unravelling the threads that make a person themselves. To those who study them, they are less a coven and more an ongoing disappearance, quietly hollowing the world one forgotten detail at a time.

Their curse is inseparable from their feeding. Each memory consumed takes something of the vampire in return. Over time, their identity begins to erode in parallel with their victims: faces blur even to allies, voices lose their distinctiveness, and names fail to anchor themselves. In time, even other vampires struggle to recall whether a Lethra was ever present at all. The more powerful they become, the more reality resists their existence—paintings fade, records degrade, and testimony collapses into uncertainty, leaving only the unsettling certainty that something has been lost, though no one can quite remember what.

Aegis Coven

Stats

  • Start with Dark Target trait

  • Start with 1 extra vampire trait

  • Start with Vampire Basics

  • Languages: Basic, Vampiric

  • Size: Medium

The Aegis Coven, they are the bound enforcers of vampire law, sworn to the Council through supernatural oaths that manifest as the Aegis Mark. Once a target is designated, the mark awakens, and the vampire becomes irrevocably linked to their quarry—able to sense them instinctively across distance and time, driven by an unbroken compulsion to pursue until the task is complete.

Aegis gain strength from the certainty of their oath, making them resilient, focused, and difficult to deceive while bound to a hunt. However, they cannot abandon a designated target or reinterpret their purpose; pursuit is absolute. To be named by the Aegis is not merely to be hunted, but to be condemned to an ending that will inevitably arrive.

Sylva Coven

Stats

  • Start with Animal Feeder trait

  • Start with Vampiric Charm trait

  • Start with Vampire Basics

  • Languages: Basic, Vampiric

  • Size: Medium

The Sylva Coven are vampires who have turned away from human blood, choosing instead to survive on the blood of animals only. Unlike other covens, they refuse to see humans as prey, forming deep emotional bonds with them.

The Sylva are defined by attachment. They fall in love easily, often deeply, and inevitably with those who cannot endure what they are. Every relationship is temporary by nature, and every bond is destined to end in ageing, loss, or separation. While they are physically weaker than most covens, they are among the most socially adaptive and emotionally perceptive, capable of reading human behaviour with unnerving accuracy. Their curse is not hunger, but continuity—being forced to live forever within the cycle of connection and loss, never able to fully escape either.

Eidolon Coven

Stats

  • Start with Sickly Feeder trait

  • Start with Holy Resistance trait

  • Start with Vampire Basics

  • Languages: Basic, Vampiric

  • Size: Medium

The Eidolon Coven are vampires who walk the threshold between life and death, often mistaken in legend for angels or visions seen at passing. They do not hunt the living but instead those whose bodies are failing but whose minds still linger. Through dreamlike intrusion and subtle communion, they ease fear, dull pain, and guide the dying towards a peaceful release.

The Eidolon believe their purpose is not to preserve life, but to perfect its ending. They feed only from the sick and dying, taking fragments of pain and fear as sustenance. In doing so, they are unaffected by holy symbols or rites of protection, as they are seen by many cultures as neither cursed nor blessed, but inevitable.

Ossuary Coven

Stats

  • Start with Corpse Feeder trait

  • Start with Acidic Breath trait

  • Start with Vampire Basics

  • Languages: Basic, Vampiric

  • Size: Medium

The Ossuary Coven are the keepers of vampire society’s darkest secrets. While other covens hunt, rule, or hide, the Ossuary ensure that nothing remains to expose them. They feed exclusively upon the dead, sustaining themselves on blood long after life has left the body. In return for their services, they are granted access to battlefields, morgues, crime scenes, and places of tragedy, where they quietly erase evidence of vampire activity and ensure that the ignorance of humanity endures.

Ossuary believe that death is a responsibility. They are experts in concealment, disposal, and the rituals surrounding the dead, moving through human society unseen. Though their methods are unsettling even to other vampires, their role is indispensable. Every coven relies upon them, and few are willing to consider what would happen if they ever decided to stop cleaning up after everyone else’s mistakes.

Werewolves

This section covers the different types of werewolf a player can become during the game. GMs can change these as they see fit. The Packs are not made to fit a specific theme and can be used in any.

Werewolf Basics

Every werewolf gets the following, unless their Pack says otherwise.

Stats

  • Rank 1 in Brawn, Agility or Health ability (choose one)

  • Claws and Fangs: natural Light Melee weapons that scale with you, using the same table as vampires (see Vampire Basics)

  • Supernatural senses: Advantage on Senses rolls to hear and see

  • Cursed blood: immune to poison and disease, and Advantage on rolls to resist curses

  • Beast flesh: resistant to weapons that are not silver

  • Silver: silver weapons deal critical damage to you

  • Hunger for flesh

  • You age very slowly, at half the rate of your regular race

  • You still need to eat, drink and sleep like regular humans. You can eat raw food without setbacks, and it takes much longer for alcohol to affect you

Lunaris Pack

Stats

  • Start with Lunar Influence trait

  • Start with Lunaris Beast Form trait

  • Start with Werewolf Basics

  • Languages: Basic, 1 extra

  • Size: Medium

The Lunaris Pack are the traditional werewolves bound to the lunar cycle. Their transformations are governed by the phases of the moon, with full transformation and peak power only achieved under the full moon.

They embody the structured curse as it was originally known: disciplined, hierarchical, and deeply tied to ancient rites. However, their reliance on lunar cycles leaves them vulnerable outside of it, making them predictable compared to other strains.

Lycan Pack

Stats

  • Start with Unbound Beast trait

  • Start with Lycan Beast Form trait

  • Start with Werewolf Basics

  • Languages: Basic, 1 extra

  • Size: Medium

The Lycan Pack are werewolves whose connection to the beast never fades. They can transform at will, but the beast within them is always awake, even in human form.

They are powerful, aggressive, and unpredictable, but suffer from emotional volatility and sudden violent outbursts. Silver is especially dangerous to them, amplifying both physical harm and loss of control. Among werewolves, they are feared for their instability and pitied for their inability to ever find peace.

Crescent Pack

Stats

  • Start with Unbound Beast trait

  • Start with Cresent Beast Form trait

  • Start with Werewolf Basics

  • Languages: Basic, 1 extra

  • Size: Medium

The Crescent Pack are werewolves born from an incomplete bond with the beast. They cannot achieve full transformation, manifesting only partial traits such as claws, fangs, eyes, and heightened senses.

Because they remain close to human form, they are often used as scouts, spies, and intermediaries between packs and human society. While this makes them valuable, many resent being treated as tools rather than full werewolves. Their curse is permanence in incompleteness—never fully beast, never fully human.

Fenrir Pack

Stats

  • Start with Forever Beast trait

  • Start with Fenrir Beast Form trait

  • Start with Werewolf Basics

  • Languages: Basic, 1 extra

  • Size: Medium

The Fenrir Pack are werewolves permanently locked in a compact beast form. They remain only slightly larger than human, allowing them to conceal themselves with clothing, masks, and integration into human environments.

They retain intelligence and speech, but their instincts are always dominant beneath the surface. Their minds are layered over the beast rather than separate from it, creating constant internal tension. They are feared not for their size, but for the unsettling truth that the beast does not need to be large to be absolute.

The Returned

This section covers the different types of Returned a player can become during the game. GMs can change these as they see fit. The Returned are not made to fit a specific theme and can be used in any.

Returned Basics

Every Returned gets the following, unless their type says otherwise.

Stats

  • Rank 1 in any one ability (choose one)

  • Wound-proof: you ignore the penalty of your first Wound

  • Dead flesh: resistant to weapons that are not silver or enchanted

  • Magic has Advantage against you, and you have Disadvantage on rolls against magic

  • Unstable death: dying makes your character an unstable spirit that is no longer under your control (see Death and Survival)

  • You do not need to eat, drink or sleep

Memora

Stats

  • Start with Ghost trait

  • Start with Possession trait

  • Start with Returned Basics

  • Languages: Basic, Dead Text

  • Size: Medium

The Memora are the dead who return through attachment, regret, or unfinished business. Having left their physical bodies behind, they exist as incorporeal beings tied to places, people, or memories they cannot abandon. They drift unseen through the world of the living, reliving fragments of their former lives while struggling to accept that they no longer belong among the living.

The Memora live upon remembrance itself, drawing strength from memories, emotions, and the lingering bonds that anchor them to existence. As those connections fade, so too do they. Their greatest fear is not destruction, but being forgotten, for a Memora without memory is a soul destined to disappear forever.

Vindica

Stats

  • Start with Dark Target trait

  • Start with 1 extra Returned trait

  • Start with Returned Basics

  • Languages: Basic, Dead Text

  • Size: Medium

The Vindica are the dead who return through hatred, obsession, or vengeance. Unlike other Returned, they reclaim their own bodies, forcing life back into flesh that was never meant to move again. Though they retain their memories and personalities, death leaves its mark, and their bodies slowly deteriorate with every passing year.

The Vindica exist for a singular purpose. Whether driven by revenge, justice, or obsession, they cannot truly rest until their goal is fulfilled. Among the Returned, they are the most feared, for a Vindica denied its purpose becomes increasingly unstable, transforming from a person seeking closure into a force consumed entirely by its own hatred.

Corvus

Stats

  • Start with Fated Look trait

  • Start with Destined Outcome trait

  • Start with Returned Basics

  • Languages: Basic, Dead Text

  • Size: Medium

The Corvus are the dead who return through sacrifice, injustice, or stolen destiny. Unlike the Vindica, they return largely intact, walking among the living as though they had never died at all. Yet every Corvus knows their return is temporary, granted only so long as the purpose that brought them back remains unfinished.

The Corvus are often viewed as omens, appearing where fate has been disrupted or where a great wrong demands correction. They retain their humanity more completely than any other Returned, but this comes with its own curse: they remember exactly what they lost, and they know that once their purpose is fulfilled, they must face death once more.

Vacua

Stats

  • Start with Walking Corpse trait

  • Start with Unwounded trait

  • Start with Returned Basics

  • Languages: Basic, Dead Text

  • Size: Medium

The Vacua are the dead who return through despair, fear, or abandonment. Something essential failed to return with them, leaving behind beings who remember fragments of who they once were but can never fully reclaim their former selves. Their bodies bear the marks of death, and their minds are haunted by an emptiness they cannot explain.

The Vacua are the most tragic of the Returned. Neither fully alive nor truly dead, they wander in search of meaning, identity, and belonging. Some cling desperately to their former lives, while others surrender to the void within them. Of all the Returned, they alone understand that survival is not always a gift.

Death and Survival

Creature How it dies and returns
Vampires A vampire only stays dead if it is killed using something holy, if its body is burned, or if its head is removed. Failing all of that, a vampire can rise again if it ingests any blood
Werewolves Werewolves can be killed in any way. However, if silver is not used to strike the final blow or on the corpse afterwards, the beast heals the mortal body and there is a chance the werewolf will come back
Returned A Returned cannot die. They can be killed for a short time, which ends with them becoming a vengeful, uncontrollable spirit. The only way to truly remove them from the mortal realm is to help them move on to the afterlife, or to perform an exorcism and banish them
Magic Blood magic or sacrificial magic can bring people back from the dead. It always costs another life or something similar, like a soul. It can also heal illness and injury, but that too requires a similar offering, such as inflicting an injury or giving an illness to another

Traits

Traits act as skills or abilities that characters learn or gain during the game. Some traits are available from the start, such as a racial trait or the traits you pick at level 1. These are what your character has learned before the game.

Your character gains traits as you level up, or may gain a trait in the game depending on the type of game you are playing. Most games give 1 trait every level.

If no trait fits what you want for your character, consider reworking an existing one, or talk to your GM and see if a new one can be made.

The creature traits for vampires, werewolves and the Returned are detailed below. Each trait uses the card-format rules and prerequisites shown in its entry.

Vampire traits

All vampire traits require you to be a vampire. The Vorago and Aegis Covens may take their ‘1 extra vampire trait’ from this list or from Control Animal (Vampire).

VT-001 Blueblood

Category: Vampire Trait · Ranked: No · Prerequisite: House Dracul

Your blood is ancient and your station is known to those who matter.

You gain Advantage on Presence rolls made against mortals.

Your Blood Point cap increases by 2.

VT-002 Vampiric Presence

Category: Vampire Trait · Ranked: No · Prerequisite: Vampire · Range: Your zone · Cost: 3 Blood Points (+3 every 3 levels) · Duration: 1 minute or 3 turns

Your presence turns the air cold. Each creature of your choice in your zone must make a Smarts roll against DS 12 + your Presence Rank.

On a fail, it is Frightened for the duration. On a pass, it is not affected and cannot be affected by this trait again for a day.

VT-003 Abyssal Hunger

Category: Vampire Trait · Ranked: No · Prerequisite: Vorago Coven

Your power rises and falls with your Blood Points. Your state depends on how full you are, compared with your Blood Point cap.

State Blood Points Effect
Starving 1 or less Disadvantage on Smarts and Presence rolls
Stable Above 1, up to half your cap No effect
Empowered Above half your cap, below your cap +2 damage with Claws and Fangs
Frenzied At your cap +4 damage with Claws and Fangs. Combat Defence -2. You must attack the nearest creature each turn, friend or foe

Frenzy ends when you drop below your cap.

VT-004 Dream Feeder

Category: Vampire Trait · Ranked: No · Prerequisite: Lethra Coven

You can feed on a sleeping creature without biting it. Gain Blood Points as normal for feeding on that creature.

The creature wakes with no memory of the last hour and does not know it was fed on. You can use this trait on the same creature once per day.

VT-005 Mind Wipe

Category: Vampire Trait · Ranked: No · Prerequisite: Lethra Coven · Range: 1 zone · Cost: 2 Blood Points (+2 every 3 levels) · Duration: 1 minute or 3 turns

You attempt to erase a creature’s memory of you. It must make a Smarts roll against DS 12 + your Presence Rank.

On a fail, the creature forgets you for the duration. It cannot make Opportunity Actions against you, and your first attack against it has Advantage. The effect ends early if you attack it.

Out of combat, a fail also erases up to 10 minutes of the creature’s memory of your choice.

VT-006 Dark Target

Category: Vampire or Returned Trait · Ranked: No · Prerequisite: Aegis Coven or Vindica

You are sworn to a hunt. Choose one creature as your Dark Target.

  1. You always know the direction and rough distance to it.

  2. You gain Advantage on attacks against it and on rolls to track it.

  3. You cannot choose another Dark Target until the first is dead, or the GM says your purpose has ended.

  4. Compulsion. If a full day passes without you moving towards or acting against your Dark Target, you gain 1 Fatigue.

VT-007 Animal Feeder

Category: Vampire Trait · Ranked: No · Prerequisite: Sylva Coven

You feed on animals only. Feeding on a beast grants Blood Points equal to its Health Rank +1 (minimum 2).

You gain no Blood Points from feeding on a humanoid.

VT-008 Vampiric Charm

Category: Vampire Trait · Ranked: No · Prerequisite: Sylva Coven · Range: 1 zone · Cost: 3 Blood Points (+3 every 3 levels) · Duration: 1 minute

You attempt to charm a humanoid. When you do so, it must make a Smarts roll.

On a fail, it is charmed by you for the duration. On a pass, the humanoid is not charmed and knows you tried.

If in combat, the creature always passes the roll.

VT-009 Sickly Feeder

Category: Vampire Trait · Ranked: No · Prerequisite: Eidolon Coven

You feed on the sick and dying. You may feed, with no roll needed, on a creature that is at or below half its HP, or that is Poisoned or diseased. Gain Blood Points equal to its Health Rank +1.

You gain no Blood Points from healthy creatures.

A creature you feed on this way feels no pain and is not Frightened by you.

VT-010 Holy Resistance

Category: Vampire Trait · Ranked: No · Prerequisite: Eidolon Coven

You are unaffected by holy items and rites of protection. You ignore the Holy items weakness, so holy items cannot make you Frightened and holy water does you no harm.

All your other weaknesses still apply.

VT-011 Corpse Feeder

Category: Vampire Trait · Ranked: No · Prerequisite: Ossuary Coven

You feed on the dead. You may feed on a creature that died within the last hour. Gain Blood Points equal to its Health Rank.

You gain no Blood Points from living creatures.

VT-012 Acidic Breath

Category: Vampire Trait · Ranked: No · Prerequisite: Ossuary Coven · Range: 1 zone · Cost: 2 Blood Points (+2 every 3 levels)

You exhale a spray of acid at a zone in range. Each creature in the zone must make an Agility roll against DS 12 + your Presence Rank, and takes 5 acid damage on a fail or half on a pass. Damage goes up by 2 every 3 levels.

Armour hit by this trait loses 1 tier of durability. Used on a corpse, it dissolves the body in 1 minute and costs nothing.

Werewolf traits

All werewolf traits require you to be a werewolf. The Packs share one Beast Form rule, and each pack changes it. The numbers match the Werewolf Basics in the main book.

Werewolf claws and fangs

Werewolves use the same table as vampires. Damage is set by the rank of the ability you roll, and goes up by 1 every 3 levels (at levels 3, 6 and 9).

Ability rank 1 2 3 4
Bite damage 4 5 6 7
Claw damage 3 4 5 6
Beast Form

Each Pack gives a different Beast Form trait. The table shows how they compare.

Trait How you enter How long it lasts Bonuses Cost
Lunaris Beast Form Interaction 1 minute or 3 turns, once per day. All night under the full moon +2 natural damage, +1 rank in Brawn and Health, Large Cannot use items or speak
Lycan Beast Form Free Action Until you choose +2 natural damage, +1 rank in Brawn and Agility, Large Cannot use items or speak. Volatile (see Unbound Beast)
Crescent Beast Form Free Action Until you choose +1 natural damage, Advantage on Senses rolls, stays Medium None
Fenrir Beast Form Always on Permanent +2 natural damage, +1 rank in Health, stays Medium Cannot change back

A rank bonus never takes an ability above Rank 4.

WT-001 Lunar Influence

Category: Werewolf Trait · Ranked: No · Prerequisite: Lunaris Pack

Your power follows the moon.

  • Full moon. You may stay in Beast Form all night, and you gain +1 rank in Agility (max 4).

  • New moon. You cannot use Beast Form, and you have Disadvantage on Brawn rolls.

  • Other nights have no effect.

WT-002 Lunaris Beast Form

Category: Werewolf Trait · Ranked: No · Prerequisite: Lunaris Pack · Action: Interaction · Duration: 1 minute or 3 turns, once per day

You change into a great wolf. While in Beast Form:

  • Your size is Large (Speed 2 zones).

  • You gain +1 rank in Brawn and Health (max 4).

  • Claws and Fangs deal +2 damage.

  • You cannot use items or speak.

Under the full moon this lasts all night (see Lunar Influence).

WT-003 Unbound Beast

Category: Werewolf Trait · Ranked: No · Prerequisite: Lycan Pack or Crescent Pack

The beast is always awake. You gain Advantage on Senses rolls to track by scent, and on Presence rolls to frighten.

Volatile. Whenever you take a Wound, or fall to half your HP or lower, you must make a Health roll against DS 10. On a fail you must attack the nearest creature, friend or foe, on your next turn.

WT-004 Lycan Beast Form

Category: Werewolf Trait · Ranked: No · Prerequisite: Lycan Pack · Action: Free Action

You change at will, and stay changed until you choose to stop or you fall to 0 HP. While in Beast Form:

  • Your size is Large (Speed 2 zones).

  • You gain +1 rank in Brawn and Agility (max 4).

  • Claws and Fangs deal +2 damage.

  • You cannot use items or speak.

WT-005 Crescent Beast Form

Category: Werewolf Trait · Ranked: No · Prerequisite: Crescent Pack · Action: Free Action

You change only in part: claws, fangs, eyes and senses. Your size stays Medium, and you can speak, use items and wear armour.

While in Beast Form:

  • Claws and Fangs deal +1 damage.

  • You gain Advantage on Senses rolls, including in combat.

You may change back at will.

WT-006 Forever Beast

Category: Werewolf Trait · Ranked: No · Prerequisite: Fenrir Pack

You are permanently locked in a compact beast form. You cannot change back.

Hiding what you are is possible with clothing and a mask, but a creature that sees you unmasked treats you as a beast, and you have Disadvantage on Presence rolls against it.

Instincts. If you take a Wound, you must make a Health roll against DS 10 or attack the nearest creature on your next turn.

WT-007 Fenrir Beast Form

Category: Werewolf Trait · Ranked: No · Prerequisite: Fenrir Pack

Your compact beast body is always in effect. You keep your intelligence and speech.

  • Your size is Medium.

  • You gain +1 rank in Health (max 4).

  • Claws and Fangs deal +2 damage.

Returned traits

All Returned traits require you to be Returned. Vindica may take their ‘1 extra Returned trait’ from this list.

RD-001 Ghost

Category: Returned Trait · Ranked: No · Prerequisite: Memora · Action: Interaction · Duration: 1 minute or 3 turns, once per combat

You become incorporeal. While in this state, you can neither deal nor take damage, and cannot touch or be touched by anything corporeal. Silver weapons, enchanted weapons and magic can still harm you.

Anchored. Choose an anchor with your GM: a place, a person or a memory. If you are more than a mile from it, you gain 1 Fatigue every hour.

RD-002 Possession

Category: Returned Trait · Ranked: No · Prerequisite: Ghost · Range: Touch · Duration: 10 minutes, once per day

You attempt to take over a creature’s body. It must make a Smarts roll against DS 12 + your Presence Rank.

On a fail, you control it for the duration, using its body and abilities but your own mind. On a pass, nothing happens.

If in combat, the creature always passes the roll.

When the possession ends, or the body falls to 0 HP, you are ejected and gain 1 Fatigue. The creature knows it was possessed.

RD-003 Fated Look

Category: Returned Trait · Ranked: No · Prerequisite: Corvus · Range: 2 zones · Duration: Once per combat

You glimpse how a creature’s story ends. It must make a Smarts roll against DS 12 + your Presence Rank.

On a fail, it has Disadvantage on its next 2 rolls. On a pass, it is not affected and cannot be affected by this trait again for a day.

Out of combat, you may ask the GM how the creature is likely to meet its end, and receive a short answer.

RD-004 Destined Outcome

Category: Returned Trait · Ranked: No · Prerequisite: Corvus · Action: Opportunity Action, once per day

You change the outcome of a roll, either yours or an ally’s, and make it a critical success.

The roll must further the purpose that brought you back. Agree your purpose with your GM when you take this trait.

RD-005 Walking Corpse

Category: Returned Trait · Ranked: No · Prerequisite: Vacua

Your body is already dead.

  • Critical hits against you deal normal damage, not doubled.

  • You do not need to breathe.

  • You ignore the Disadvantage from Fatigue levels 1 and 2.

  • Hollow. Healing you receive from potions, spells and rests heals you for half, rounded up.

RD-006 Unwounded

Category: Returned Trait · Ranked: No · Prerequisite: Vacua

You ignore the rank penalty from your first two Wounds, instead of only your first. The penalty from a third Wound still applies, and Wounds still count towards death.

Balance Notes

This edition was balanced against the Star Wars Theme and the Core System. Everything below differs from the original deck. Each row can be reverted on its own.

Changes

Area Change
Claws and Fangs Damage was 4 to 7 (bite) and 3 to 6 (claw) plus the full character level. That reached 14 or more by level 7, against 7 for a Heavy weapon. It now keeps the base numbers and adds 1 every 3 levels. Natural weapons count as one weapon for the Light tag
Vampire Basics Enhanced Brawn, Agility and Health became Rank 1 in one of them. Immunity to curses became Advantage against curses. Senses became Advantage on Senses rolls. Resistance to non-silver weapons, the weaknesses table, Blood Points and the 4 hour sleep are unchanged
Werewolf Basics The deck gave werewolves the same resistance and immunities as vampires with no weakness. Silver now deals critical damage, as in the old Fantasy lycanthropy rules. Enhanced attributes and curses changed the same way as for vampires
Returned Basics Immunity to Wound penalties became ignoring the penalty of the first Wound, like Star Wars droids that take Wounds as normal. Immunity to non-silver, non-enchanted weapons became resistance. Added Rank 1 in one ability
Text Descriptions are as written. Only spelling was fixed (ingest, vengeful, sacrificial, alcohol, distributed)

Open questions

Item Note
Trait cards The coven, pack and type traits (Blueblood, Dream Feeder, Lunar Influence, Ghost and the rest) are not in the Ability Database, and the deck only names them. They could not be balanced here
Werewolf numbers The deck has no werewolf damage table. Werewolves use the vampire table as an assumption
Crescent Pack It starts with Unbound Beast, the same trait as the Lycan Pack. This may be a copy-paste slip. The trait name Cresent is kept as written
Blood Points The deck says Blood Points are priced 1-for-1 against Magic. A cap of 2 to 8 Blood Points against spells costing 20 to 100 Magic does not match that, so the wording was kept and the cost scale needs your decision
Weakness table The deck text gave the weaknesses as one list. I read Silver as getting worse at levels 1, 2 and 3+, and every other weakness as applying at all levels. Check this against the slide
Art The original deck had art that did not come through, so this edition has none

Part III — Modern Theme

The modern setting for the After World Core System.

Races · Other changes

Introduction

The Modern Theme adds a modern setting to the Core System (Part I): races, period-based backgrounds, themed traits, equipment and chemistry, plus two optional Feature Modules. Everything in Part I still applies unless this Part says otherwise.

To start a Modern campaign, the GM chooses a year or period. Players then make characters using the races, backgrounds and equipment in this Part, and the traits and powers in the Ability Database (Part V). The GM chooses any Feature Modules they want.

Rules Precedence

Core System → Modern Theme → Feature Module → GM house rule. When two rules disagree, the more specific book wins. See Rules Precedence in Chapter 11.

What Is in This Part

Section What it holds
Choosing a Period How the GM sets the year or period of the campaign.
Races, Backgrounds, Traits, Inventory, Chemistry The standard Modern content every game can use.
Enhancements Optional Feature Module: three tiers of body and technology upgrades.
Superpowers Optional Feature Module: powers and how they grow.

Theme-specific content

The races and traits in this Theme are made for a modern setting. That can include sci-fi, apocalyptic or superhero games. They may only be chosen in a campaign that uses this Theme, because they may not be balanced for other Themes.

Every weapon and armour should still work in mixed-Theme play, at the GM’s discretion. A GM may also allow a race or trait from this Theme in another Theme, but should think about it more carefully.

Choosing a Period

The GM chooses any year or period for the campaign. Some examples are the 1980s, the 1990s, the 2000s, the present day, a post-apocalyptic world, a science-fiction future and an alternate history. The period changes what people carry and what the world looks like. It does not change the rules.

Races and traits are the same in every period. The period affects backgrounds, equipment and the feel of the setting. See Backgrounds below for how starting equipment changes with the period.

Races

Choose a lineage that shapes who you are, what you can do and where you belong in the modern world. The tables and text give each race the same kind of stats: a starting trait or power, an ability rank, a perk, languages and a size.

Races are tuned to the same power level as the Star Wars species. A race gives you one starting trait or power, Rank 1 in one ability, one perk, languages and a size. A strong perk or immunity comes with a limit or a weakness. Where a line says or 1 extra trait, you may take any trait instead.

Human

Stats

  • Rank 1 in any one ability of your choice
  • Start with 1 extra trait
  • Adaptable: once per day, reroll one ability roll and keep the new result
  • Languages: 1 extra language of your choice
  • Size: Medium

Humans are the most widespread people in the world. Their societies are many and varied, and they have no single shared culture. Their history, environment and upbringing shape them far more than any one rule, so a human character can come from anywhere and be anyone.

Martian

Stats

  • Start with Armour Mastery trait

  • Start with Handyman trait

  • Rank 1 in Smarts ability

  • Sealed suit: you start with a sealed Reinforced Armour (Light 2) suit with a battery pack, in place of your starting armour

  • Earth-bound: you must wear a sealed suit outside a Martian atmosphere or gain 1 Fatigue per hour

  • Languages: Martian, Serpent Tongue

  • Size: Medium

Martians are a red-skinned humanoid people who began as humans abducted by the Elder Drakes and taken to Mars for an experiment. They built a technocratic society where intellectuals rule, and emotion is outlawed because nuclear war turned their green world into red wastes. They are tall, thin, very smart and physically weak, with orange-red skin, red eyes and bleach-white hair.

Vulrocian

Stats

  • Start with Terrakinesis power (Tier 1)

  • Rank 1 in Brawn or Health ability

  • Stone-blooded: immune to poison and disease, and resistant to fire

  • Vulnerable to water

  • Languages: Elemental, 1 extra

  • Size: Medium or Large (choose when you make your character)

Vulrocians are an old race of elementals created by the Ancients to guard a sacred relic, the Heart of the Lands. They are honour-driven warriors who value command over the land above all, with telekinetic powers that work only on stone. They are covered in molten volcanic rock with lava veins, and their blood is fire. The males look gorilla-like and strong, and the females are tall and more slender.

Thalor

Stats

  • Start with Aquakinesis power (Tier 1)

  • Rank 1 in Brawn or Health ability

  • Fluid body: immune to poison and disease, and resistant to water and ice

  • Vulnerable to fire

  • Languages: Elemental, 1 extra

  • Size: Medium or Large (choose when you make your character)

The Thalor are a fluid elemental species from high-pressure oceanic worlds. Their bodies are a cohesive liquid mass held together by internal pressure fields, so they are stable but adaptive rather than true shapeshifters. They look like semi-translucent humanoids of dark water, with void-black eyes, gill structures along the neck and ribs, and edges that dissolve into droplets and reform. Like the Vulrocians, they were made to protect a relic, the Heart of the Waters.

Elder Drake

Stats

  • Rank 1 in Presence ability

  • Start with Shapeshifting power (Tier 1)

  • Serpent blood: resistant to poison

  • Languages: Serpent tongue, 1 extra

  • Size: Medium

Elder Drakes are advanced snake-like humanoids who see themselves as gods and introduce themselves that way. They keep armies of slaves abducted from many worlds and are responsible for many races’ existence. Their scales come in many colours, with green to orange, blue or red eyes. Some have two legs and some a coiled body, and most are tall, though shapeshifting makes it hard to tell.

Voryn

Stats

  • Rank 1 in Brawn, Agility or Health ability

  • Start with Flight power (Tier 1)

  • Gravity-born: Advantage on Brawn rolls to lift, push or hold, and resistant to fall damage

  • Languages: Vorynish, 1 extra

  • Size: Medium

The Voryn are an apex humanoid species from the high-gravity world of Veydris. They look human, with pale skin, white hair and icy blue eyes, but their control over gravity and mass gives them great strength, speed, flight and durability, and they project gravitational energy from their eyes. Their society is organised by gravitational control, and their blood holds compressed gravitational energy that is dangerous to them if exploited.

Mutant

Stats

  • Rank 1 in Brawn, Health or Senses ability

  • Wasteland-born: immune to radiation, and resistant to poison and disease

  • Regeneration: once per middle rest, as an Interaction, heal HP equal to your Health Rank x2

  • Languages: Any 2

  • Size: Medium

Mutants are physically changed, with features such as extra limbs, glowing eyes, rough skin or unnatural growths. Some are larger or stronger than normal humans and others are frail but have strange abilities. They are often feared by other people, so most live in isolated communities in the wasteland or in ruined cities, valuing survival, loyalty and strength.

Robot

Stats

  • Rank 1 in Brawn or Health ability

  • Machine body: immune to poison, disease and radiation

  • Vulnerable to electricity: electric damage is critical damage, and the DS of any electric Stun effect against you is raised by 2

  • Recharge: you do not sleep. You must spend 4 hours a day plugged into power or gain 1 Fatigue for every day without. A recharge counts as a middle rest

  • Takes Wounds as normal

  • Languages: Binary, 1 extra

  • Size: Small, Medium or Large (choose when you make your character)

Robots are made of metal, plastic or advanced composites, and may be humanoid or built for a specific task. They do not age or eat, but they need power sources. They tend to value logic, order and efficiency, though those with evolved minds may show curiosity, creativity or emotion. Relations with humans are mixed, and robots often struggle with questions of purpose, freedom and identity.

Hybrid

Stats

  • Rank 1 in any one ability

  • 1 racial trait from any race

  • 1 trait or power of your choice

  • Languages: Any 2

  • Size: Small, Medium or Large

Hybrids are rare individuals born from the union, natural or experimental, of two different species. Most are unstable and short-lived, but a few become viable, powerful and stable. These True Hybrids are among the most unpredictable beings on Earth, and because of the Meta-Gene’s flexibility, hybrids can occur between almost any two races.

Backgrounds

A background is the profession or life you led before the adventure. It gives you tools, training and a starting kit. The eight professions below are the same in every period. What changes with the period is the equipment, which is listed after the professions. The GM decides which professions suit the period they have chosen.

Professions

Soldier

You are dedicated to an organisation or army and have committed to uphold the tenets of your chosen allegiance. You have a set of skills acquired in this role. People know you as a hero or villain and your name carries weight, or you are not known at all and are off grid.

  • Trained in: Soldier tools, all weapons, all armour

  • Soldier tools: anything used for battle planning, scouting enemies, driving and other military acts

  • Equipment: Heavy, flashlight, soldier rank, camo gear, repair kit, night vision goggles, dirt bike

Scoundrel

You know the underbelly of society and many of its less reputable members. Playing by the rulebook is against everything you know to be true, and the only rules that matter are the ones that put you ahead. You are no stranger to violence, theft and other crime, though you may or may not be a criminal yourself.

  • Trained in: Scoundrel tools, light weapons, armour with Bulk 0 to 2

  • Scoundrel tools: anything used for lockpicking, forgery, disguises and other criminal and roguish acts

  • Equipment: Heavy, extra carry pouches (+10), 5 glow sticks, lockpicking kit, forgery kit, climbing gear, caltrops, disguise kit, 100 ft of rope, 10 ft of wire, 1 smoke grenade, grappling hook

Scientist

You have studied life’s greatest mysteries and scour the world for knowledge only a select few possess. You learn quickly, read between the lines and solve the hardest puzzles. Your obsession may be biology, physics or chemistry, and you know your field inside and out.

  • Trained in: Scientist tools, light weapons, armour with Bulk 0 to 3, chainsaw

  • Scientist tools: anything used for research, medicine, experimentation and other scientific acts

  • Equipment: Medium, stack of paper, 10 pens, book of study, 10 pills, unique science instrument, 10 ft of copper wire, stethoscope

Botanist

You know how to harness the natural world to make soothing medicines and potent poisons. Plants, flowers and fungi are your surroundings, and you know them better than people. You can bring people back from the brink with a few crushed leaves, and many pay for your concoctions and knowledge.

  • Trained in: Botanist tools, light weapons, armour with Bulk 0 to 2, chemistry

  • Botanist tools: anything used for brewing medicines, poisons, herbs and other alchemical acts

  • Equipment: Heavy, 5 medicines, medical equipment, stack of paper, 10 pens, 4 prosthetic limbs, brewing equipment, mortar and pestle, sack of 10 plants for brewing, 10 sticks of incense

Hunter

You know the wild and can track the creatures that roam it. You spend more time around your prey than people, know traps and weaponry, and are no stranger to sleeping rough for weeks to follow a pack or a lone predator. You are a trained killer even when you choose not to kill.

  • Trained in: Hunter tools, all weapons, all armour, traps

  • Hunter tools: anything used for traps, tracking, foraging and other animal and nature acts

  • Equipment: Medium, binoculars, night vision goggles, camo gear, a large net, 5 sets of traps, climbing gear, 100 ft of rope, caltrops, 20 bait, world map, unique item from prey x3

Craftsman

You have a trade and you are good at it. You can make weapons, armour, traps, structures and everyday items, and many will know your work from your maker’s mark. Working with weapons and armour means you are used to them and can tell when they need work.

  • Trained in: Craftsman tools, all weapons, all armour, all vehicles

  • Craftsman tools: anything used for repairing, forging, constructing and other crafting and tinkering acts

  • Equipment: Heavy, repair kit, blacksmithing kit, item materials (20), flashlight, hammer, anvil, portable forge, bellows, sack of 100 coals, masonry kit, electrical kit, carpentry kit

Celebrity

You are wealthy and influential. You know the political world and can move through grand events and private meetings far better than a battlefield. You know people with stock in money-making ventures and have sway in those circles, and a clear advantage in social and formal settings.

  • Trained in: Celebrity tools, light weapons, cloth and non-metal armour

  • Celebrity tools: anything used for city management, writing, linguistics and other political and social acts

  • Equipment: Heavy, stack of paper, 10 pens, and 1 of: entertainment gear, political gear, sports gear or tech gear (ask your GM what these might be)

Explorer

You have travelled the known world, sailing the oceans and climbing the tallest peaks. You know cultures far beyond where you were born, can read the weather before it changes, and treat maps like a second language. You know how to survive in the wild and where to make camp.

  • Trained in: Explorer tools, light weapons, armour with Bulk 0 to 2, gauntlets, whips

  • Explorer tools: anything used for navigation, cartography, weather reading and other survival and travelling acts

  • Equipment: Heavy, binoculars, flashlight, grappling hook, preservation kit, night vision goggles, compass, wristwatch, climbing gear, 200 ft of rope, world map, winter gear

Equipment by Period

Your background gives you the same training and tools in every period. The equipment line is adapted to match the world. The present-day lists are shown with the professions above. Each item keeps its purpose, and the GM may adapt any item further.

Alternate History

For an alternate history, start from the list of the nearest period above and adapt it to the technology of your world. Keep each item’s purpose.

Traits

Traits act as skills or abilities that characters learn or gain during the game. Some are available from the start, such as a racial trait or the traits you pick at level 1. These are what your character has learned before the game.

Your character gains traits as you level up, or may gain a trait in the game depending on the type of game you are playing. Most games give 1 trait every level.

If no trait fits what you want for your character, consider reworking an existing one, or talk to your GM and see if a new one can be made.

The traits are in the Ability Database, with the core and combat traits.

Inventory

Your inventory is the equipment, supplies and personal effects you carry while surviving in the After World. All mechanics for weapons, armour, carry weight and equipment weights stay in the Core System. This chapter only gives modern examples that use those profiles without changing them.

In the modern world, inventory is shaped by practicality, mobility and scarcity. A survivor might carry a battered rucksack of scavenged ammunition, bottled water, duct tape and a sidearm from an abandoned police station. Firearms need maintenance, batteries run dry and fuel is precious. A cracked smartphone with no signal, a faded photograph or a scavenged ID badge can say as much about a character as their skills.

When managing equipment, always follow the Core System. In a conflict, the Core System takes precedence.

Armour and shields

For full statistics see the Core System’s Armour Profiles.

Weapons

Weapons in the modern age emphasise range, lethality and reliability, though many are damaged or poorly maintained. Ammunition is finite and noise attracts attention. For attack resolution, damage, ranges and weapon tags, always refer to the Core System.

Chemistry

Chemistry works the same as the Core System crafting system. A medicine or poison can heal or damage, or apply an effect for a set duration. An effect not listed as damage, such as paralysis or blindness, uses half of the regular value as damage or lasts for the listed duration.

Level DS Damage or effect Range Duration
Novice 8 8 1 zone 3 turns
Adept 9 10 2 zones 4 turns
Master 11 15 3 zones 5 turns

Feature Modules

Feature Modules are optional. The GM switches on the ones they want and tells the players before character creation. If both modules are used, a character may begin with a power from either, as described in each module.

Feature Module: Enhancements

Enhancements are upgrades to the body or its equipment, such as implants, augmentations and technology. This Feature Module is optional.

In this module: Starting With a Module · How Enhancements Work

Starting With a Module

Modules replace the old Origin choice. If the GM uses only Superpowers, a character chooses one starting power. If the GM uses only Enhancements, a character chooses one starting Enhancement. If the GM uses both, a character chooses one from either.

How Enhancements Work

Enhancements are arranged in three tiers. A character may choose an Enhancement at the start of the game or later, and may take one as a normal level-up choice. Enhancements have their own limits, separate from those of other powers, and tiers progress as the character does.

Enhancements are scientific modifications that improve a character’s capability, from chemicals to cybernetics. They suit super soldiers and mutants. An Enhancement that gives a power-like effect can be used once per combat without cost, for 1 minute or 3 turns.

Enhancement Description
Strength Get Enhance Brawn, once per combat without cost
Speed Get Enhance Speed, once per combat without cost
Health Get Enhance Health, once per combat without cost
Sponge Once per combat, take half damage from the next firearm hit
Wildman Get Foresight trait and +1 rank in Senses
Brainiac Get Crafter trait and +1 rank in Smarts
Egghead Get Mortar & Pestle and Historian traits

Feature Module: Superpowers

Superpowers are extraordinary abilities. This Feature Module is optional.

In this module: Starting With a Module · Aquakinesis

Starting With a Module

If the GM uses only Superpowers, a character chooses one starting power. If the GM uses both modules, a character chooses one starting power or Enhancement.

All powers are in the Ability Database. A few rules to remember:

  • All powers use Tiers 1 to 3 with no point costs.

  • When chosen, a power starts at Tier 1 and goes up one Tier each time you pick it at a level up. Picking a power takes the place of a trait.

  • You can Over Channel (Tier 4 and above) to push beyond normal limits. Each Tier above 3 gives you 1 Fatigue.

  • Attacks use 1d10 + the attribute Skill die against the target’s Combat Defence. Resisted effects make the target roll against your Power Defence.

Aquakinesis

Category: Superpower

Control and shape water.

Tier 1 – 4 Water damage, single target. Push 1 zone. Roll: 1d10 + Senses against Combat Defence.

Tier 2 – 6 damage, Cone (your zone). Creates difficult terrain.

Tier 3 – 9 damage, Burst (target zone). Restrained on a failed roll against Power Defence.

Tier 4 – 12 damage, Burst (2 zones). Creates cover or flooding.

Tier 5 – 15 damage. Reshape the whole area, which becomes hazardous. Fatigue per extra Tier.

Weakness: Ineffective without water nearby, or in dry places.

Appendix: Balance Notes

Races

Race Change
Arabian Angel Outlaw trait replaced with Foresight
Martian Starting powered armour replaced with a sealed Reinforced Armour suit. Needing a suit on Earth is now 1 Fatigue per hour, like the Kel Dor mask
Vulrocian Immunity to fire changed to resistance. Vulnerable to water added. Terrakinesis is a Tier 1 power
Thalor Immunity to water and ice changed to resistance. Vulnerable to fire added. Aquakinesis is a Tier 1 power
Elder Drake Immunity to poison changed to resistance. Seeing through smoke removed
Voryn The extra power from the first two pages of Superpowers removed. Added a gravity perk
Mutant Immunity to poison and disease changed to resistance. Healing is once per middle rest instead of unlimited
Robot No Wound drawbacks removed. Takes Wounds as normal, vulnerable to electricity and needs a recharge, like Star Wars droids
Hybrid Start with 1 trait or power instead of 2, plus the racial trait
Human peoples The Westerner, Asian, Arabian and African descriptions were rewritten, because the originals leaned on stereotypes of real groups

Other changes

Area Change
Movement Enhanced and Skilled move 2 zones once per combat instead of always
Enhancements Strength, Speed, Health and Sponge are now once per combat, because permanent versions were stronger than a trait. Brainiac gives Crafter and +1 rank in Smarts instead of Crafter and Handyman. Egghead is unchanged
Weapons Chainsaw and Minigun damage lowered to 7. Heavy pistol and PDW or carbine moved from Light Ranged to Medium Ranged
Armour Armour examples now name their Core Armour Profile
Chemistry DS, range and duration now match the Star Wars Chemistry and Medicine table
Aquakinesis New. The original Thalor needed it but it was not in the Ability Database, so it mirrors Terrakinesis. Please check it
Backgrounds Wording tidied, no mechanical change. They match the Star Wars equivalents
Art The race portraits and background icons are not in this edition

The race portraits and background icons are not in this edition

Introduction

This Part adds a setting to the After World Core System and takes the Fantasy Theme into a galaxy far, far away. It is built as a set of swappable modules, so a table switches on only the pieces its campaign needs.

All Core System rules still apply unless a module says otherwise. Everything here is a guideline, not a rule: re-flavour it, trim it, or swap a module out mid-campaign.

How to build a campaign

  • Take SW-BASE. It is always on. It holds species, backgrounds, traits guidance, gear and medicine.

  • Pick one Era module. ERA-OR (Old Republic), ERA-RT (Republic Twilight), ERA-CW (Clone Wars) or ERA-GE (Galactic Empire). An Era sets tone, factions, era backgrounds and which gear is common.

  • Pick any Feature modules. FT-FORCE, FT-SHIPS, FT-DROIDS and FT-SCUM each bolt on one system and can be mixed freely.

  • Tell the players what is on. Player options come only from switched-on modules.

Precedence

When two rules disagree, the more specific book wins. The order, weakest to strongest, is:

Core System → SW-BASE → Era module → Feature module → GM house rule

This mirrors the Fantasy Theme, where the Theme overrides the Core System when they contradict.

How a module is written

Every module opens with a card in the same shape, so you can see at a glance what it needs and what it changes.

Field Meaning
Requires Modules that must be on.
Pairs well with Suggested companions.
Conflicts Modules that should not run together without GM care.
Overrides Core or SW-BASE rules the module changes.
Adds What players and GMs get.

Using content across themes

As in the Fantasy Theme, every weapon and armour here is fully functional in any After World campaign at the GM’s discretion. Species and traits are tuned to this setting, so allow them elsewhere only after considering balance.

Unofficial fan work

Star Wars names, species and settings belong to their respective owners. This is a non-commercial fan adaptation of After World and is not affiliated with or endorsed by them. All rules text and stat blocks are original.

Module Map

There are eleven modules: one base, four Eras, four Features and two appendices. Pick one Era, then add whichever Features fit the story.

Code Module Requires Pairs well with Conflicts Adds
SW-BASE Base Core System Ev erything No ne Sp ecies, backgrounds, trait guide, gear, and medicine, stims and compounds
ERA-OR Old Republic SW-BASE FT-FORCE, FT-SHIPS ERA-RT, ERA-CW, ERA-GE Ancient Jedi and Sith, Force orders, vibro-age gear
ERA-RT Republic Twilight SW-BASE FT-SCUM, FT-SHIPS, FT-FORCE ERA-OR, ERA-CW, ERA-GE Corruption conspiracy, Exposure and Evidence, Frontier Law, clans, two backgrounds
ERA-CW Clone Wars SW-BASE FT-FORCE, FT-DROIDS, FT-SHIPS ERA-OR, ERA-RT, ERA-GE Clone troopers, battle droids, Jedi generals, war rules
ERA-GE Galactic Empire SW-BASE FT-SCUM, FT-SHIPS ERA-OR, ERA-RT, ERA-CW Rebels, Imperial officers, Heat and Reputation rules
FT-FORCE The Force SW-BASE ERA-OR, ERA-CW None Force Pool, Light and Dark alignment, powers, lightsabers
FT-SHIPS Starships and Vehicles SW-BASE Any Era None Ship stats, crews, space zones, vehicle combat
FT-DROIDS Droids and Companions SW-BASE ERA-CW None Playable droids, droid companions, upgrades
FT-SCUM Bounties and Syndicates SW-BASE ERA-GE None Bounty board, Fame and Infamy, crime syndicates
APP-LOOT Loot Tables SW-BASE Any None Treasure and gear by level 1 to 7
APP-CONVERT Conversion Sheet None Any None Fantasy-to-Star-Wars cross reference

Eras conflict with each other because each one redefines which species, gear and factions are common. A legacy campaign that spans two eras should use one Era module at a time and swap between acts.

Ready-made presets

Preset Modules Feel
Jedi Knight SW-BASE, ERA-OR, FT-FORCE Order-versus-order drama with sabres and ancient ruins
Generals’ War SW-BASE, ERA-CW, FT-FORCE, FT-DROIDS, FT-SHIPS Large-scale war with clones, droids and Jedi leaders
Rebellion SW-BASE, ERA-GE, FT-SHIPS, FT-DROIDS Hit-and-run resistance under an empire
Scum and Villainy SW-BASE, ERA-GE, FT-SCUM, FT-SHIPS Smugglers, bounty hunters and syndicate politics
Street Level SW-BASE, ERA-GE Gritty, Force-free survival on one world
Hidden Force SW-BASE, ERA-GE, FT-FORCE A few hunted Force users in a hostile galaxy
Republic Twilight SW-BASE, ERA-RT, FT-SCUM, FT-SHIPS, FT-FORCE Corruption, syndicates and clans on the edge of a failing Republic

SW-BASE: Species

Module card
Code SW-BASE (always on)
Requires Core System
Overrides Replaces the Fantasy Theme races chapter. Fantasy races stay available only if the GM allows them.
Adds Twelve core species, forty-one Legends species, four Near-Human peoples, species variants and a custom species rule

Choose a species that shapes your character’s abilities, culture and place in the galaxy. Each entry lists starting traits, ability ranks and languages in the same format as the Fantasy Theme. Where a line says or 1 extra trait, you may take any trait instead of the bonus.

Galactic Basic is known to every species here. Droid player characters are in FT-DROIDS.

Human

Stats

  • Rank 1 in any one ability of your choice

  • Start with 1 extra trait

  • Adaptable: once per day, reroll one ability roll and keep the new result

  • Languages: Basic, 1 extra

  • Size: Medium

Humans are the most widespread people in the galaxy and live on every kind of world, from glittering Core cities to frontier moisture farms. They have no shared culture, only a habit of fitting in wherever they land.

Twi’lek

Stats

  • Start with Gift Of The Gab trait

  • Rank 1 in Presence ability

  • Lekku sense: Advantage on Senses rolls to read a speaker’s mood

  • Languages: Basic, Ryl

  • Size: Medium

Twi’leks come in every skin colour and are known for the two head-tails, or lekku, that shape their speech and body language. Many are traders, dancers and diplomats, and their home world’s harsh climate has taught them to be shrewd.

Zabrak

Stats

  • Start with Healthy trait

  • Rank 1 in Health ability

  • Tolerance: Advantage on Health rolls against poison and pain effects

  • Languages: Basic, Zabraki

  • Size: Medium
