Zabraks are recognised by the ring of horns across the head and by a stubborn will to endure. They are proud of surviving what would break others and are often found as soldiers, mercenaries and monks.

Wookiee

Stats

  • Start with Shoulders Of Giants trait

  • Rank 1 in Brawn ability

  • Wookiee Rage: you may take Rampage without its 1 Wound prerequisite

  • Languages: Basic (understood, rarely spoken), Shyriiwook

  • Size: Large

Wookiees are towering, long-lived tree-dwellers covered in thick fur, with a deep sense of loyalty and a famously short temper when a friend is hurt. They are brilliant climbers and skilled builders of bowcasters and treetop cities.

Rodian

Stats

  • Start with Target trait

  • Rank 1 in Senses ability

  • +1 to hit and damage with all light ranged weapons or 1 extra trait

  • Languages: Basic, Rodese

  • Size: Medium

Rodians are green-skinned hunters from a jungle world where tracking and killing prey is a cultural art. Hunters of bounties, beasts and rival clans, they have a sharp eye for a target and a long memory for a debt.

Togruta

Stats

  • Start with Pincer trait

  • Rank 1 in Agility ability

  • Montral sense: you can sense creatures in the dark inside your Melee Zone without light

  • Languages: Basic, Togruti

  • Size: Medium

Togruta are striped, horned hunters whose people work as a close pack. They trust the group before the individual and are happiest fighting at a friend’s shoulder.

Mon Calamari

Stats

  • Start with Handyman trait

  • Rank 1 in Smarts ability

  • Amphibious: breathe air and water, and swim at normal movement speed

  • Languages: Basic, Mon Calamarian

  • Size: Medium

Mon Calamari are amphibious people from an ocean world, famed as shipwrights and engineers. They are thoughtful, idealistic and fiercely committed to freedom, and they build as well as they lead.

Bothan

Stats

  • Start with Gray Fox trait

  • Rank 1 in Smarts ability

  • Spy Network: once per day, automatically succeed on one roll to find rumours, contacts or secrets

  • Languages: Basic, Bothese

  • Size: Medium

Bothans are furred and expressive, and their clans treat information as the only currency worth hoarding. Their politics are subtle and their spies are everywhere.

Sullustan

Stats

  • Start with Heart Of Oak trait

  • Rank 1 in Senses ability

  • Cave-born: you can see through any type of darkness

  • Languages: Basic, Sullustese

  • Size: Medium

Sullustans live in vast cave cities and have an uncanny sense of direction. Their big dark eyes see where others cannot, and their talent for navigation makes them favourites among pilots and scouts.

Trandoshan

Stats

  • Natural claws: Light Melee, Slashing, 3 damage, Agility or Brawn

  • Rank 1 in Brawn ability

  • Regenerative: after a full rest, heal 1 extra injury

  • Languages: Basic, Dosh

  • Size: Medium

Trandoshans are scaled reptilian hunters who treat the hunt as a sacred rite and sell their skills to anyone with credits. They shed old wounds as easily as old skin.

Jawa

Stats

  • Start with Handyman trait

  • Start with Standing In Shadow trait or Crafter trait

  • Rank 1 in Smarts ability

  • Scrapper: Advantage on rolls to appraise, haggle for or salvage used gear

  • Languages: Basic, Jawaese

  • Size: Small

Jawas are small hooded scavengers who roam deserts in great crawlers and strip anything they find for parts. They are cunning traders with sharp ears and quick fingers.

Kel Dor

Stats

  • Start with Foresight trait

  • Rank 1 in Senses ability

  • Mask-bound: you must wear a breath mask outside your home atmosphere or gain 1 Fatigue per hour

  • Languages: Basic, Kel Dor

  • Size: Medium

  • With FT-FORCE on: start with 10 extra Force Points

Kel Dor are calm, masked people from a world of helium-rich air, and many follow a tradition of quiet study and spiritual discipline. They are seen as wise advisers and dangerous when their patience ends.

Species Variants

These can be chosen in place of the standard entry to reflect your character.

Variant Stats
Half-Breed 1 trait from main species. 1 rank and 1 trait from secondary species. Languages: Basic and secondary species. Size: main species.
Offworld Born Choose any species entry, but replace its first trait with 1 extra trait of your choice and take Rank 1 in an ability different from the species’ own.
Custom Species Rank 1 in 1 ability and 2 traits. Languages: Basic and 1 extra. Size set by GM. Agree the concept with your GM first.

SW-BASE: More Species

This chapter adds forty-one species from the wider Legends galaxy. Every one first appeared before The Clone Wars TV series (2008), and nothing from that show or later is used. They use the same format as the core species and are tuned to the same power level, so a table can mix them freely with the twelve above.

Species are listed alphabetically. Era modules say which are common, and the GM decides whether any other species is present in the campaign. The Species Variants above still apply: Half-Breed and Offworld Born work with any entry here. Where a perk says a creature is Charmed, Mind Trick and similar powers count.

Aqualish

Stats

  • Start with Pincer trait

  • Rank 1 in Brawn ability

  • Thick-skulled: Advantage on Health rolls to resist being Stunned

  • Languages: Basic, Aqualish

  • Size: Medium

Aqualish are tusked, wide-eyed natives of the ocean world Ando, with a reputation for short tempers and rough company. Many work as miners, pirates and bar-room muscle, and they settle most disputes with their fists.

Barabel

Stats

  • Start with Weapon Mastery trait

  • Rank 1 in Brawn ability

  • Scaled hide: your skin counts as Light Armour (Light 1) with no Armour Penalty and Bulk 0. Worn armour replaces it

  • Languages: Basic, Barabel

  • Size: Medium

Barabels are big, dark-scaled reptilians from a scorching world, honour-bound and slow to forgive. They are formidable hunters and warriors who respect strength and hold a grudge for life.

Besalisk

Stats

  • Start with Handyman trait

  • Rank 1 in Brawn ability

  • Four arms: your Interaction attack may use any one-handed weapon, not only a Light weapon

  • Languages: Basic, Besalisk

  • Size: Medium

Besalisks are broad, four-armed people from Ojom, famous for their big appetites, long lives and talents as cooks, smiths and tradespeople. A hard-working Besalisk can do the work of two.

Bith

Stats

  • Start with Crafter trait

  • Rank 1 in Smarts ability

  • Keen ears and nose: Advantage on Senses rolls that use hearing or smell

  • Languages: Basic, Bith

  • Size: Medium

Bith are pale, bulbous-headed people from Clak’dor VII with extremely sharp hearing and smell. Their calm, orderly society produces scientists, engineers and the musicians who play in cantinas across the galaxy.

Cathar

Stats

  • Start with Close The Distance trait

  • Rank 1 in Agility ability

  • Pounce: Advantage on the first attack you make in each combat

  • Languages: Basic, Cathar

  • Size: Medium

Cathar are lion-like people with manes and sharp reflexes. They are proud, fiercely loyal warriors, and many carry a deep sense of loss for a homeworld scarred by war.

Cerean

Stats

  • Start with Historian trait

  • Rank 1 in Smarts ability

  • Binary mind: once per day, reroll one Smarts roll and keep the new result

  • Languages: Basic, Cerean

  • Size: Medium

Cereans are tall beings with elongated, conical skulls that hold a second brain, which lets them weigh decisions with calm logic. They are respected as philosophers, diplomats and counsellors.

Chagrian

Stats

  • Start with Senator trait

  • Rank 1 in Presence ability

  • Amphibious: breathe air and water, and swim at normal movement speed

  • Languages: Basic, Chagri

  • Size: Medium

Chagrians are blue-skinned amphibious people from Champala, with a pair of horns sweeping back from the head. Their culture prizes politics and ritual, and Chagrian officials appear in many halls of government.

Chiss

Stats

  • Start with Foresight trait

  • Rank 1 in Smarts ability

  • Tactician: once per combat, as an Opportunity Action, give one ally Advantage on one roll

  • Languages: Basic, Cheunh

  • Size: Medium

Chiss are blue-skinned, red-eyed people from the Unknown Regions, organised into powerful ruling families. They are cool strategists who study an enemy’s art and habits to find a weakness, and few ever leave home.

Clawdite

Stats

  • Start with Master Of Disguise trait

  • Rank 1 in Presence ability

  • Shapeshifter: as a Full Action, take the appearance of any Medium humanoid you have seen. It lasts until you end it (free) or fall unconscious. Your clothing and gear do not change

  • Languages: Basic, Clawdite

  • Size: Medium

Clawdites are reptilian shapeshifters from Zolan who can reshape their bodies to resemble others. Many work as spies, assassins and infiltrators, and they live uneasily among people who distrust their talent.

Defel

Stats

  • Start with Someone There? trait

  • Rank 1 in Agility ability

  • Wraith-skinned: Advantage on hiding rolls in dim light or darkness. In bright light you have Disadvantage on Senses rolls to see unless you wear goggles

  • Languages: Basic, Defel

  • Size: Medium

Defels are furred, fanged people whose coats absorb visible light, so most look like moving shadows. They see best in ultraviolet and make natural scouts, spies and burglars.

Devaronian

Stats

  • Start with Criminal Scum trait

  • Rank 1 in Agility ability

  • Free-roaming: Advantage on rolls to find passage, lodging or a fence in a spaceport

  • Languages: Basic, Devaronese

  • Size: Medium

Devaronians are red-skinned, horned wanderers. Many roam the galaxy as traders, drifters and scoundrels, and they are known for their charm and their wandering spirit.

Dug

Stats

  • Start with Fast Hands trait

  • Rank 1 in Agility ability

  • Four-limbed sprint: +1 zone of movement. You cannot move this way while holding a Heavy weapon

  • Languages: Basic, Dugese

  • Size: Small

Dugs are small, flat-nosed people who walk on their hands as easily as their feet and move in a wild scramble. They are quick, loud and used to being underestimated, and many are racers, scavengers and brawlers.

Duros

Stats

  • Start with Heart Of Oak trait

  • Rank 1 in Smarts ability

  • Star-charts: Advantage on Smarts rolls to navigate in space and to recall star systems and hyperspace routes

  • Languages: Basic, Durese

  • Size: Medium

Duros are thin, green-skinned people with red eyes, born to travel. They have a long tradition of navigation, piloting and starship design, and almost every port has a Duros pilot or chart-reader.

Ewok

Stats

  • Start with Standing In Shadow trait

  • Rank 1 in Senses ability

  • Forest-wise: Advantage on Senses and Agility rolls to hide, track and climb in forests

  • Languages: Basic (understood, rarely spoken), Ewokese

  • Size: Small

Ewoks are small, furred forest-dwellers from Endor who live in treetop villages. They are fearless fighters with a gift for traps and gliders, a love of drums, and far more courage than their size suggests.

Falleen

Stats

  • Start with Attractive Intelligence trait

  • Rank 1 in Presence ability

  • Pheromones: once per day, take Advantage on a Presence roll to charm or persuade one creature within your Melee Zone. It does not work on creatures that cannot smell

  • Languages: Basic, Falleen

  • Size: Medium

Falleen are graceful, scaled people who produce pheromones that sway the minds of those nearby. Many rise to power in crime syndicates and noble houses, using patience and charm.

Gamorrean

Stats

  • Start with Shoulders Of Giants trait

  • Rank 1 in Brawn ability

  • Heavy-handed: +1 damage with Heavy Melee weapons

  • Languages: Basic (understood, rarely spoken), Gamorrese

  • Size: Medium

Gamorreans are tusked, green-skinned brutes with a clan culture built around strength. They make loyal guards and soldiers, and the best of them are far cleverer than their reputation suggests.

Gand

Stats

  • Start with Target trait

  • Rank 1 in Senses ability

  • Mask-bound: you must wear a breath mask outside your home atmosphere or gain 1 Fatigue per hour

  • Languages: Basic, Gand

  • Size: Medium

Gand are insectoid people who breathe ammonia and wear masks to survive off-world. Their findsmen follow a ritual tradition of tracking and hunting, and many Gand refuse to give their personal names to outsiders.

Geonosian

Stats

  • Start with Crafter trait

  • Rank 1 in Smarts ability

  • Winged flight: when you move you may fly, ignoring ground terrain and obstacles. You cannot fly in armour with Bulk 1 or more, or while carrying over half your carry weight

  • Languages: Basic (understood, rarely spoken), Geonosian

  • Size: Medium

Geonosians are insectoid hive-dwellers with wings and a strict caste system. Their drones are builders and engineers of huge skill, and their aristocrats rule from towering spires.

Gran

Stats

  • Start with Pincer trait

  • Rank 1 in Senses ability

  • Triple-eyed: Advantage on Senses rolls to spot hidden or camouflaged creatures and objects

  • Languages: Basic, Granish

  • Size: Medium

Gran are short, stocky, three-eyed people who love communal living and thrive in groups. Their wide vision makes them keen observers, and a Gran is rarely far from friends.

Gungan

Stats

  • Start with Evasive Action trait

  • Rank 1 in Agility ability

  • Amphibious: breathe air and water, and swim at normal movement speed

  • Languages: Basic, Gungan

  • Size: Medium

Gungans are tall amphibious people with long ears and bright voices, who live in bubble cities beneath the lakes of Naboo. They are brave, clannish warriors with electropoles and energy shields, whatever their reputation for clumsiness.

Hutt

Stats

  • Start with Towering Terror trait

  • Rank 1 in Presence ability

  • Slug body: your speed is halved, and you cannot climb or jump

  • Mind-shielded: Advantage on Smarts rolls to resist being Charmed or having your mind read

  • Languages: Basic, Huttese

  • Size: Large

Hutts are huge, slug-like beings from Nal Hutta who run crime syndicates across the Outer Rim. They are cunning, patient and cruel, and their power rests on wealth and fear. A Hutt player character is unusual and should be agreed with the GM first.

Iktotchi

Stats

  • Start with Foresight trait

  • Rank 1 in Senses ability

  • Precognition: once per combat, as an Opportunity Action, give one attack against you Disadvantage

  • Languages: Basic, Iktotchese

  • Size: Medium

Iktotchi are horned people from a harsh, stormy moon, gifted with limited precognition. The gift makes them excellent warriors and advisers, and many keep a stern, watchful outlook.

Ithorian

Stats

  • Start with Druid trait

  • Rank 1 in Presence ability

  • Bellow: once per combat, as a Main Action, creatures in your Melee Zone make a Health roll (DS 11) or are Stunned until the end of their next turn

  • Languages: Basic, Ithorese

  • Size: Medium

Ithorians, often called Hammerheads, are gentle, long-necked herders who revere nature and live on floating herd ships. Their two-throated voices carry far, and a peaceful Ithorian can still unleash a thunderous bellow.

Kaminoan

Stats

  • Start with Mortar & Pestle trait

  • Rank 1 in Smarts ability

  • Clinical eye: Advantage on Smarts rolls to identify injuries, poisons and diseases

  • Languages: Basic, Kaminoan

  • Size: Medium

Kaminoans are tall, pale, long-necked people of an ocean world, masters of genetic science and cloning. They are polite, composed and detached, and bring a cool cleverness to anything they study.

Kubaz

Stats

  • Start with Someone There? trait

  • Rank 1 in Senses ability

  • Sensitive snout: Advantage on Senses rolls that use smell

  • Languages: Basic, Kubazian

  • Size: Medium

Kubaz are long-snouted people who eat insects and speak in soft, quick voices. Many serve as spies and informants who listen at doors and follow rumours, and they blend into crowds with ease.

Mirialan

Stats

  • Start with Deflecting Dance trait

  • Rank 1 in Agility ability

  • Graceful: Advantage on Agility rolls to balance, tumble and dance

  • Languages: Basic, Mirialan

  • Size: Medium

Mirialans are green-skinned people who wear ritual tattoos marking life events and great deeds. Disciplined and athletic, many become dancers, duelists or members of the Jedi Order.

Muun

Stats

  • Start with Gray Fox trait

  • Rank 1 in Smarts ability

  • Banker’s eye: Advantage on Smarts rolls to appraise value, spot forgeries and balance accounts

  • Languages: Basic, Muun

  • Size: Medium

Muuns are tall, pale, long-fingered people from Muunilinst, whose banks hold fortunes across the galaxy. They are cold, patient and brilliant with numbers, and few outsiders see past their poise.

Nautolan

Stats

  • Start with Heart Of Oak trait

  • Rank 1 in Agility ability

  • Amphibious: breathe air and water, and swim at normal movement speed

  • Languages: Basic, Nautila

  • Size: Medium

Nautolans are green-skinned amphibious people whose head-tentacles sense moods and movement in the water. They are sociable and fearless, and many fight as swimmers, sailors and pilots.

Neimoidian

Stats

  • Start with Tactical Retreat trait

  • Rank 1 in Smarts ability

  • Shrewd bargainer: Advantage on rolls to haggle, appraise or hire

  • Languages: Basic, Neimoidian

  • Size: Medium

Neimoidians are pale, mournful-faced people with sharp trade instincts and a marked fear of danger. Many dominate corporations and trade fleets from behind layers of staff and hired guards.

Nikto

Stats

  • Start with Healthy trait

  • Rank 1 in Brawn ability

  • Desert-hardy: Advantage on Health rolls against heat and thirst

  • Languages: Basic, Nikto

  • Size: Medium

Nikto are scaled, horned people with tough hides and desert grit. Many serve as guards, skiff crews and hired muscle for crime lords.

Ortolan

Stats

  • Start with Distracting Presence trait

  • Rank 1 in Presence ability

  • Keen ears: Advantage on Senses rolls that use hearing

  • Languages: Basic, Ortolan

  • Size: Small

Ortolans are blue-skinned, trunk-nosed people with big ears and a deep love of food and music. They are gentle and sociable, and a skilled Ortolan musician can make or break a cantina.

Pau’an

Stats

  • Start with Towering Terror trait

  • Rank 1 in Presence ability

  • Low-light vision: you can see in dim light without penalty

  • Languages: Basic, Utapese

  • Size: Medium

Pau’ans are tall, grey-skinned, sunken-eyed people who live in the sinkhole cities of Utapau. They are solemn, disciplined and calm, and their severe looks make others uneasy.

Quarren

Stats

  • Start with Close The Distance trait

  • Rank 1 in Smarts ability

  • Amphibious: breathe air and water, and swim at normal movement speed

  • Languages: Basic, Quarrenese

  • Size: Medium

Quarren are squid-faced amphibious people who share their ocean world with the Mon Calamari, and they do not always agree with them. They are skilled swimmers and practical, cautious dealers.

Selkath

Stats

  • Start with Mortar & Pestle trait

  • Rank 1 in Smarts ability

  • Amphibious: breathe air and water, and swim at normal movement speed

  • Languages: Basic, Selkath

  • Size: Medium

Selkath are lizard-like amphibious people with a calm, orderly culture centred on the ocean world of Manaan. They hold fiercely to neutrality and are famed for their healing medicines.

Sith Pureblood

Stats

  • Start with Distracting Presence trait

  • Rank 1 in Presence ability

  • Dark heritage: Advantage on Health rolls against Dark powers and poisons

  • Languages: Basic, Sith

  • Size: Medium

  • With FT-FORCE on: start with 10 extra Force Points

Sith purebloods are red-skinned, tendril-faced people of the original Sith species, once ruled by dark-side sorcerers. Few survive in the wider galaxy, and those who do are proud, rare and often met with fear or awe.

Toydarian

Stats

  • Start with Gift Of The Gab trait

  • Rank 1 in Agility ability

  • Winged flight: when you move you may fly, ignoring ground terrain and obstacles. You cannot fly in armour with Bulk 1 or more, or while carrying over half your carry weight

  • Mind-shielded: Advantage on Smarts rolls to resist being Charmed or having your mind read

  • Languages: Basic, Toydarian

  • Size: Medium

Toydarians are small, snouted, winged traders from Toydaria, whose quick wings keep them aloft. They are shrewd haggling traders and are well known for being immune to many mental tricks.

Tusken Raider

Stats

  • Start with Weapon Mastery trait

  • Rank 1 in Brawn ability

  • Desert-born: Advantage on Health rolls against heat and thirst, and no Disadvantage in sandstorms

  • Languages: Basic (understood, rarely spoken), Tusken

  • Size: Medium

Tusken Raiders are masked nomads who roam the desert wastes of Tatooine in tribes. They are fierce and territorial and live by their own customs, with wrappings and masks that guard them against sun and sand.

Ugnaught

Stats

  • Start with Handyman trait

  • Rank 1 in Smarts ability

  • Cloud-born: Advantage on Health rolls against poison gas and thin air

  • Languages: Basic, Ugnaught

  • Size: Small

Ugnaughts are small, pig-faced people who work as engineers, miners and tinkerers in gas mines and cloud cities. They are industrious, sturdy and used to dangerous work.

Verpine

Stats

  • Start with Crafter trait

  • Rank 1 in Smarts ability

  • Hive-link: speak silently with other Verpine within 1 zone

  • Languages: Basic (understood, rarely spoken), Verpine

  • Size: Medium

Verpine are thin, insectoid people who grow in hive-like colonies and share a quiet, radio-like link. They are famed for their skill with machines, ships and weapons.

Weequay

Stats

  • Start with Backstab trait

  • Rank 1 in Agility ability

  • Silent code: Advantage on rolls to read body language and to resist interrogation

  • Languages: Basic, Weequayan

  • Size: Medium

Weequay are leathery-skinned people with braided hair, from a harsh world. They speak largely through scent and gesture, and many work as pirates, guards and hired muscle.

Whiphid

Stats

  • Start with Healthy trait

  • Rank 1 in Brawn ability

  • Cold-hardy: Advantage on Health rolls against cold and exposure

  • Languages: Basic, Whiphid

  • Size: Large

Whiphids are huge, tusked, furred hunters from a frozen world. They are skilled trackers and cold-weather fighters who prize a good hunt above all.

SW-BASE: Near-Human Peoples

In Legends the Echani and Miraluka are Near-Human, and Dathomirians are Humans. Mandalorians are a warrior culture rather than a species, and are treated here as a Near-Human people. None of them is a separate species here. Use one of these in place of the Human entry. You count as Human for any rule that mentions Humans, and you do not get Adaptable or the free extra trait. Every one of them first appeared before The Clone Wars TV series (2008).

Echani

Stats

  • Start with Black Belt trait

  • Rank 1 in Agility ability

  • Duellist’s eye: Advantage on Senses rolls to read an opponent’s fighting style or next move

  • Languages: Basic, Echani

  • Size: Medium

The Echani are pale-skinned, white-haired, silver-eyed Near-Humans from Eshan. Their culture prizes efficiency and the art of the duel, and they rank each other by fighting skill. Echani blades and martial arts are respected across the galaxy.

Miraluka

Stats

  • Start with Foresight trait

  • Rank 1 in Senses ability

  • Force-sight: you see through the Force instead of with eyes. You cannot be Blinded and darkness does not hinder you, but you cannot read text or screens, and you only perceive inanimate details inside your Melee Zone

  • Languages: Basic

  • Size: Medium

  • With FT-FORCE on: start with 10 extra Force Points

Miraluka are Near-Humans born without eyes, who perceive the world through the Force. Many wear veils or visors, blend easily into Human society, and become Jedi, seers and scholars.

Dathomirian Witch

Stats

  • Start with Druid trait

  • Rank 1 in Presence ability

  • Hard-living: Advantage on Health rolls against exhaustion, heat and poison

  • Languages: Basic

  • Size: Medium

  • With FT-FORCE on: start with 10 extra Force Points

Dathomirians are Humans from a harsh, primal world where clans led by Force-wise witches hold their own territories. They are fierce, independent and skilled in the wilds, and many ride and tame beasts that would terrify anyone else.

Mandalorian

Stats

  • Start with Armour Mastery trait

  • Rank 1 in Health ability

  • Unbowed: Advantage on Health rolls against fear, intimidation and being Stunned

  • Languages: Basic, Mando’a

  • Size: Medium

Mandalorians are Near-Human warriors from Mandalore, bound together by a code of honour, a shared language and the armour they wear into every fight. Raised to hunt, to fight and to keep their word, they hold that a clan is made of those who stand beside you, and they are respected and feared as soldiers and mercenaries across the galaxy.

SW-BASE: Backgrounds

Backgrounds are your character’s profession or knowledge before the game starts. Pick one, or make your own by choosing one tool set and training, or talk to your GM about a new one.

The Fantasy Theme rules for backgrounds carry over unchanged. Being untrained in an item or task gives Disadvantage on rolls involving it. Each background’s tools give +3 to rolls for the tasks they are used for, but this never applies to attack or damage rolls. Disadvantage does apply to weapons you are not trained in.

Era modules add extra backgrounds. SW-BASE backgrounds work in every Era.

Smuggler

You know the underside of the galaxy and many of its less reputable people. Rules are a suggestion, and the only ones that matter are those that keep you ahead. You may or may not be a criminal yourself.

  • Trained in: Smuggler tools, light weapons, armour with Bulk 0 to 2

  • Smuggler tools: anything used for lockpicking, slicing, forgery, disguise and other criminal or roguish acts

  • Equipment: Heavy, extra carry pouches (+10), 5 glow rods, electro-pick kit, slicer kit, forgery kit, climbing gear, 100 ft of cable, disguise kit, 1 smoke grenade, grappling hook

Soldier

You served in an army, fleet or militia and pledged yourself to its cause. Your discipline and rank carry weight with other fighters. Your unit’s code is something you can keep or break, but never ignore.

  • Trained in: Soldier tools, all weapons, all armour

  • Soldier tools: anything used for battle planning, scouting, vehicle driving, military comms and other martial acts

  • Equipment: Heavy, 5 glow rods, unit rank insignia, repair kit, extra carry pouches (+10), comlink, unit colours, basic military ride as set by the GM and Era

Mystic

You have studied or meditated on the galaxy’s deepest mysteries and scour old archives and temples for knowledge few possess. You are good at reading between the lines and solving the hardest puzzles. If FT-FORCE is on, this is the usual starting point for Force-sensitive characters, but not the only one.

  • Trained in: Mystic tools, light weapons, armour with Bulk 0 to 3, staff

  • Mystic tools: anything used for research, meditation, ritual and other mental or spiritual acts

  • Equipment: Medium, datapad, stylus, code or text of your order, 10 sticks of incense, fire starter, 10 ft of cord, galaxy map

Medic

You know how to patch bodies and brew medicines, from clean bacta patches to dirty field stims. Many pay for your treatments and seek your knowledge, and you can bring someone back from the brink with the right supplies.

  • Trained in: Medic tools, light weapons, armour with Bulk 0 to 2, stims and medicines

  • Medic tools: anything used to treat wounds, brew stims and toxins, and other medical acts

  • Equipment: Heavy, 5 medpacs, medical equipment, datapad, stylus, 4 prosthetic limbs, chem mixer, 10 bacta patches, 10 sticks of incense

Bounty Hunter

You track down people and creatures across worlds. You spend more time with your quarry than with your own kin and know a good number of them better. You are a trained killer even when you choose not to kill, and a more effective one when you must.

  • Trained in: Hunter tools, all weapons, all armour, grenades, traps

  • Hunter tools: anything used for tracking, trapping, scanning and other pursuit acts

  • Equipment: Medium, grappling hook, 1 smoke grenade, 10 glow rods, binder cuffs and a large net, 5 sets of traps, climbing gear, 100 ft of cable, caltrops, 20 tracking beacons, galaxy map, 3 trophies from past targets

Technician

You have a trade and you are good at it. You can build or fix weapons, armour, droids, vehicles and everyday items, and your maker’s mark is etched on all of it. Working with gear every day means you can tell when it needs attention.

  • Trained in: Technician tools, all weapons, all armour, traps, grenades, gauntlets, staff, double weapons

  • Technician tools: anything used for repairing, welding, constructing and other tinkering acts

  • Equipment: Heavy, repair kit, welding kit, 20 item materials, 5 glow rods, hydrospanner, portable workbench, 10 power cells, cutter kit, tinkering kit

Diplomat

You have influence, family or an office, and you know the political world. You can traverse grand halls and embassies far better than a battlefield. You have picked up persuasive tricks along the way and a seat at a fine table is never far away.

  • Trained in: Diplomat tools, light weapons, cloth and non-metal armour

  • Diplomat tools: anything used for administration, writing, linguistics and other political or social acts

  • Equipment: Heavy, datapad, stylus, forgery kit, electro-pick kit, disguise kit, official credentials or seal, city map, galaxy map, 1 unique item

Spacer

You have travelled the known galaxy, crossing void lanes and hostile worlds. You know cultures beyond the one you were born into and can read hyperspace charts like a second language. You are no stranger to sleeping rough in a cargo bay.

  • Trained in: Spacer tools, light weapons, armour with Bulk 0 to 2, gauntlets, whips

  • Spacer tools: anything used for navigation, cartography, reading weather and conditions, and other survival and travel acts

  • Equipment: Heavy, electro-pick kit, 10 glow rods, grappling hook, ration and preservation kit, extra carry pouches (+10), compass and nav chip, chronometer, climbing gear, 200 ft of cable, galaxy map, environment suit

SW-BASE: Traits

Traits work exactly as in the Core System, and the After World Ability Database is the master list. Nearly every database trait works in a Star Wars game with a change of flavour only, so no new rules are needed for them. This chapter shows how to read them, which Fantasy traits to swap out, and adds seven setting traits.

Reading database traits in Star Wars

Database trait Star Wars reading
Heart Of Oak (CT-018) Applies to piloting starships, speeders and skiffs as well as sailing.
Crafter (CT-007) Crafting weapons, armour and gear.
Criminal Scum (CT-008) Lockpicking and defeating physical security. Slicing uses the Slicer trait below.
Handyman (CT-017) Repairing droids, vehicles, ships and gear.
Mortar & Pestle (CT-022) Creating medical items such as stims, medpacs and antitoxins.
Druid (CT-011) Handling and taming creatures.
Shepard (CT-025) Applies to creature companions. See FT-DROIDS for droid companions.
Dire Pet (CT-010) Your companion grows into a larger, rideable beast.
Senator (CT-024) Political and legal scenes: Senate floors, courts, embassies, syndicate talks.
Strider (CT-030) Use a light or medium melee weapon, including a lightsaber, to knock aside a blaster bolt.
Shieldwall (CT-026) Works with energy shields and riot shields.
Quick Draw (RT-006) Works with blaster pistols.
No More Loading (CT-023), Quick Load (RT-007), No Ammo No Problem (RT-005) Ammo is power cells and gas cartridges. Reload and Loading work as written.
Grenadier (RT-003) Works with thermal detonators and all other grenades.
Trick Shot (RT-009) Works with net launchers and ensnare cables.
Tutored By Tolkien (CT-034) Four extra languages. Rename to Linguist if you prefer.

Fantasy traits

The six Fantasy traits (FT-001 to FT-006) depend on magic and the undead, so they are off by default.

  • FT-FORCE on: Everybody Lives, Unlimited Power and Call Of The Wild may be used with Force powers. Treat Heal as Force Heal, spells as Force powers and magic points as Force Points. Tangible and Rise need the GM’s permission.

  • FT-FORCE off: none of the six are available.

New setting traits

These use the same layout as the entries in the Ability Database (Part V). Add them to your table’s copy of the database if you wish.

The setting traits for this Theme are catalogued in the Ability Database (Part V). Each links to its full entry.

Code Trait Entry
SW-001 Slicer Slicer
SW-002 Droid Whisperer Droid Whisperer
SW-003 Jury-Rigger Jury-Rigger
SW-004 Hot Pilot Hot Pilot
SW-005 Cover Fire Cover Fire
SW-006 Dead Drop Dead Drop
SW-007 Field Medic Field Medic

SW-BASE: Inventory

Your inventory is the weapons, armour, tools and gear you carry through a galaxy of broken governments and busy spaceports. All mechanics for weapons, armour, carry weight and equipment weights stay in the Core System, and this chapter only gives Star Wars examples that use those profiles without changing them.

Blasters use power cells. One cell is one reload, so you do not track individual shots unless your GM wants a gritty game.

Armour

For full statistics see the Core System’s Armour Profiles.

Armour Profile and tier Flavour notes
Civilian clothes or robes None Everyday wear
Flight suit or flak vest Light Armour (Light 1) Pilot and scout standard
Armoured jacket Reinforced Armour (Light 2) Smuggler and bounty hunter favourite
Trooper kit Balanced Armour (Medium) Mercenary and soldier staple
Heavy trooper armour Heavy Armour (Heavy 1) Front-line infantry
Durasteel plate Full Armour (Heavy 2) Officer or elite battle armour
Powered exo-armour Powered Armour Bulk ignored when charged with a power cell
Shield Profile Flavour notes
Wrist shield Light Shield Small parrying shield
Riot shield Medium Shield Standard security and trooper shield
Blast shield Heavy Shield Full-body cover
Personal energy shield Light Shield Ignores Bulk. Works against ranged attacks only.

Melee weapons

Lightsabers and other Force weapons are in FT-FORCE.

Weapon Profile Unique tags Range Damage
Combat knife Light Melee Throwable 1 zone 3
Vibro-knife Light Melee Piercing 4
Stun baton Light Melee Crushing, DS 12 (Health roll or Stunned until end of their next turn) 2
Gaderffii Medium Melee Crushing 5
Vibroblade Medium Melee Slashing 5
Vibro-axe Medium Melee Slashing 4 (1H) / 6 (2H)
Force pike Heavy Melee Piercing, Reach 6
Vibrosword Heavy Melee Slashing 7
Electrostaff Heavy Melee Reach 3 (Attack Twice)

Ranged weapons

All blasters except Heavy Ranged and Siege weapons have a stun setting: switching it is an Interaction, and a hit deals no damage. The target makes a Health roll against DS 11 and is Stunned until the end of their next turn on a fail.

Weapon Profile Unique tags Range Damage
Hold-out blaster Light Ranged Hidden, Reload 1 zone 3
Blaster pistol Light Ranged Reload 2 zones 4
Heavy blaster pistol Medium Ranged Reload 2 zones 6
Blaster carbine Medium Ranged Reload 2 zones 5
Flame projector Medium Ranged Burning, Loading 1 zone 4
Blaster rifle Heavy Ranged Reload 3 zones 6
Bowcaster Heavy Ranged Piercing, Loading 2 zones 6 (+2 when wielded by a Wookiee)
Sniper rifle Heavy Ranged Piercing, Loading 3 zones 7
Repeating blaster cannon Siege Stationary, Loading 3 zones 18

Grenades and thrown explosives

Weapon Profile DS Effect or damage
Frag grenade Blast Thrown DS 12 6 damage, half on a pass
Thermal detonator Blast Thrown DS 13 12 damage, half on a pass
Stun grenade Debuff Thrown DS 12 Stunned on a fail
Flash grenade Debuff Thrown DS 12 Blinded on a fail
Sonic grenade Debuff Thrown DS 12 Deafened on a fail
Ion grenade Debuff Thrown DS 12 Stunned on a fail, droids and vehicles only
Smoke grenade Zone Thrown DS 13 Blinded while in the zone
Gas grenade Zone Thrown DS 12 2 damage per turn while in the zone

Crafting Examples

These are setting examples. The crafting rules themselves are in the Core System, Chapter 9.

Make a roll relevant to how you are crafting against the Difficulty Score for the item’s skill level. The material used decides which skill level is needed.

Skill level DS Typical products Material
Novice 7 Basic blasters, knives, patches, kits Scrap metal, plastoid, synthetic fibre
Adept 8 Rifles, armour, droid parts, tools Durasteel, alloy ingots, fine circuitry
Master 10 Heavy armour, powered armour, advanced droids Phrik alloy, rare crystals, prototype cores

Adept work needs a workshop and Master work needs a fully equipped lab or factory.

SW-BASE: Medicine, Stims and Compounds

The Fantasy Theme’s Alchemy chapter becomes Chemistry and Medicine. It works the same way as the Core System crafting system, but a stim, toxin or compound can heal, damage, or apply an effect for a set duration instead of making an item.

Chemistry and Medicine

Level DS Damage or effect Range Duration
Novice 8 8 1 zone 3 turns
Adept 9 10 2 zones 4 turns
Master 11 15 3 zones 5 turns

An effect not listed as damage, such as paralysis or blindness, uses half of the regular value as damage or lasts for the listed duration.

Stims, medicines and toxins

Level Item Type Effect
1-2 Medpac Medicine Heals HP equal to the user’s Health Rank +1 and treats injuries
1-2 Bacta patch Medicine Heals HP equal to the user’s Health Rank
1-2 Antitoxin vial Medicine Instantly removes the Poisoned effect
1-2 Glow gel Utility Lights a Melee Zone for 1 hour when applied to an object
1-2 Paralytic dart Toxin Target rolls Health against DS 7 or becomes Grounded
1-2 Stench capsule Toxin 1-zone cloud. Those inside have Disadvantage on Senses rolls
1-2 Scent masker Utility Advantage on Stealth against tracking for 1 hour
1-2 Soothing patch Medicine Removes 1 point of Fatigue (Level 1 Fatigue only)
1-2 Adrenaline stim Stim Instantly restores Movement Action, then 1 Fatigue after 10 minutes
1-2 Clotting powder Medicine A player at 0 HP does not take Wounds for 1 round
3-4 Plasma coating Stim Apply to a weapon for +1 Fire damage for one combat
3-4 Hardening gel Stim AR 3 but -1 CD for 1 minute or 3 turns
3-4 Reflex booster Stim +1 Agility Rank (max 4) for 1 hour
3-4 Sedative dart Toxin Target rolls Health against DS 8 or sleeps for 1 hour
3-4 Corrosive gel Toxin Permanently reduces target’s AR by 2 (non-powered armour only)
3-4 Sensor stim Stim +1 Senses Rank. Allows hearing in Outer Zones
3-4 Rage stim Stim +2 damage, but attacks against the user have Advantage
3-4 Courage stim Stim Immune to Frightened for 1 hour
5-6 Rapid bacta injection Medicine Heals 2 HP at the start of every turn for 5 turns
5-6 Cloaking spray Stim Automatic Stealth success for 1 minute
5-6 Det pack Compound 6 damage to all in a Melee Zone
5-6 Broad-spectrum antitoxin Medicine Cures Poisoned, Paralysed and Restrained
5-6 Muscle graft serum Stim +1 Brawn Rank and Large size for 10 minutes
5-6 Silent movement gel Stim Move through enemies without triggering Opportunity Actions
5-6 Haze toxin Toxin Target’s Smarts and Presence Ranks drop to 0 for 1 hour
5-6 Fortitude dose Medicine Max HP increases by the user’s Health Rank for 1 hour
7 Last stim Medicine If reduced to 0 HP, the user stabilises at max HP
7 Necrotoxin Toxin Target takes 1 Wound at the start of every turn until cured
7 Silver-tongue serum Stim All Presence rolls count as Critical Success for 1 minute
7 Chrono stim Stim The user takes two full turns in one round of combat
7 Death dose Toxin A target below 10 HP dies instantly, with no Death Rolls

With FT-FORCE on, also use Force-dampener dust (Level 3-4 toxin: Senses roll against DS 8 or lose 20 Force Points) and Force tonic (Level 5-6 medicine: instantly restores all Force Points).

ERA-OR: Old Republic

Module card
Code ERA-OR
Requires SW-BASE
Pairs well with FT-FORCE, FT-SHIPS
Conflicts ERA-RT, ERA-CW, ERA-GE
Overrides Ship travel speed, availability of heavy armour, which factions are common
Adds Two backgrounds, Order Standing, Ancient Relics, factions

The Old Republic is an age of ancient orders, buried temples and slow hyperspace lanes, when the Jedi and the Sith fight over the galaxy’s future. Armies still rely on vibroblades and armour as much as blasters, and a single sabre duel can decide a world.

Era rules

Order Standing. Track your Standing from 0 to 5 with each major group (Jedi Order, Sith Empire, Republic Military). Standing rises when you complete missions for the group and falls when you break its code. At Standing 3 or more you gain +3 to rolls to persuade members and use their facilities. At 0, members treat you as a threat.

Slow lanes. Hyperspace journeys between sectors take days rather than hours. Each full day of travel counts as a middle rest only if the ship has a bunk, a cook and a quiet crew.

Armour and shields. Personal energy shields are common (Light Shield profile, ignores Bulk). Heavy Armour and Full Armour are plentiful on every battlefield.

Ancient Relics. Treat these as Level 5+ loot. Each one is unique and carries a story.

Relic Effect
Holocron Once per day, gain Advantage on a Smarts roll about the holocron’s subject.
Sith talisman Once per day, a Dark-side power you use costs half as many Force Points. Gain 1 Dark point when you do. Needs FT-FORCE.
Jedi archive crystal Once per day, a Light-side power you use costs half as many Force Points. Gain 1 Light point when you do. Needs FT-FORCE.

Era backgrounds

Jedi Padawan

You were raised in the Order and trained by a Knight or Master, and you are sworn to its code. Your name carries weight with the Republic and with the people who fear it.

  • Trained in: Jedi tools, light weapons, lightsabers (FT-FORCE), armour with Bulk 0 to 2

  • Jedi tools: anything used for meditation, archives, diplomacy and other acts of the Order

  • Equipment: Medium, robes, lightsaber (FT-FORCE) or vibroblade, datapad, comlink, 5 glow rods, ration pack, copy of the Jedi code

  • With FT-FORCE on: start with 1 point of Light alignment

Sith Acolyte

You trained under a Sith master and learned that power is earned by taking it. You are no stranger to fear, ritual or betrayal, though you may still choose what you use them for.

  • Trained in: Sith tools, light weapons, lightsabers (FT-FORCE), armour with Bulk 0 to 3

  • Sith tools: anything used for intimidation, forbidden lore, ritual and other dark acts

  • Equipment: Medium, dark robes, lightsaber (FT-FORCE) or vibrosword, datapad, ritual relic, 5 glow rods, 10 sticks of incense

  • With FT-FORCE on: start with 1 point of Dark alignment

Without FT-FORCE, treat both as Mystic backgrounds with the Equipment shown.

Species notes

The twelve core species are common. From More Species, Cathar, Selkath, Mirialan and Sith Purebloods suit this era especially well, and the GM decides which of the rest are present. Kel Dor and Zabrak are widely known as Order members, and Twi’lek and Rodian traders are found in every spaceport.

Factions

Faction Wants Usually allied with Usually opposed to
Galactic Republic Order, trade and unity Jedi Order Sith Empire
Jedi Order Peace and the balance of the Force Republic Sith Empire
Sith Empire Conquest and domination Mercenary companies Republic, Jedi Order
Hutt cartels Profit and influence Whoever pays Anyone who threatens trade
Free Worlds Independence Traders Both great powers

ERA-RT: Republic Twilight

Module card
Code ERA-RT
Requires SW-BASE
Pairs well with FT-SCUM, FT-SHIPS, FT-FORCE (Hidden Force), FT-DROIDS
Conflicts ERA-OR, ERA-CW, ERA-GE
Overrides How far Republic law reaches, how common the Force and Jedi are, which armour and factions are common
Adds Exposure and Evidence rules, Republic and Jedi Standing, Frontier Law, Clan Standing, two backgrounds, clan gear, conspiracy tiers, factions, campaign starter

The Republic is old, rich and rotting. In the decades before open war, the Senate talks about justice while its fixers sell favours, governors take bribes from cartels, and the Jedi are stretched thin keeping a peace they cannot see the edges of. On the Outer Rim the law is a rumour. Hutt kajidics, warrior clans and syndicates rule what the Republic cannot reach, and sometimes they work for the people who say they oppose them.

This is a game of leverage, favours and proof. The party’s biggest weapon is what it can show and who it can make listen. The default time is the decades before the Clone Wars, and 45 BBY fits well.

Era rules

Frontier Law. The Republic’s reach shrinks with distance. Mark each world as Core, Colonies, Mid Rim or Outer Rim.

Region Checkpoint DS Bribery Hyperspace
Core 10 Costs one lifestyle step more than the Bribery table Charted lane (DS 8)
Colonies and Mid Rim 9 As the Bribery table Charted lane (DS 8) or rarely used (DS 10)
Outer Rim 8 Costs one lifestyle step less than the Bribery table Rarely used (DS 10) or uncharted (DS 12)

A checkpoint is a Presence or Smarts roll against the DS, with +3 for a forgery kit and Advantage for a real credential, as in ERA-GE. Heat is tracked as Local Heat from FT-SCUM, per world or sector.

Armour and weapons. Blasters are standard. Vibroweapons are common on the frontier and among clans. Heavy Armour and Full Armour are rare outside clans, security firms and elite guards. Personal energy shields are costly and rare (treat as Level 5-6 loot).

Force use. The Force is rare, and its users are mostly Jedi or hidden. Jedi are respected in Republic space and may carry a lightsaber openly, though they are expected to answer to the Order. A Force power used in public by someone who is not a Jedi adds 1 Local Heat. A Dark power used in public adds 2 Local Heat and may attract the Order’s attention. Use the Hidden Force preset to run this.

Species. The twelve core species are common, and so are most of the More Species. Prejudice exists and is local, and it is a story choice, not a rule.

Exposure and Evidence

The central track of this Era is Exposure, from 0 to 10. It measures how much proof the party holds of the corruption they are chasing. The conspiracy fights harder as the party learns more.

Evidence. The party gains Evidence by finding records, recovering recordings, protecting witnesses, taking bribes on camera, or breaking a ledger’s code. The GM gives each piece a value from 1 to 3:

Value Evidence
1 A rumour with a name, a forged order, or a witness who will only talk off the record
2 A genuine ledger page, a recording, or a witness who will testify
3 A signed order, a full ledger, or a witness who can name the inner circle

Exposure equals the total value of Evidence the party holds, to a maximum of 10. If Evidence is destroyed, stolen, discredited or a witness is killed, Exposure falls by its value. Evidence can be copied and hidden, but each copy needs a safe place and someone to hold it.

Exposure What happens
0-2 The conspiracy does not know you. Doors open as normal.
3-5 Their agents watch you. Contacts are leaned on. Add 1 Local Heat in any Core or Colonies world.
6-8 Assassins, fixers and offers arrive. Allies must be paid or protected, and Presence rolls to persuade officials have Disadvantage.
9-10 The inner circle moves in the open. The party can bring them down, or be buried by them.

Breaking the web. The conspiracy has three tiers (see below). To bring down a tier, the party needs Exposure equal to or above the tier’s number and a way to use it: a hearing, a broadcast, a Jedi inquiry or a vote. Each tier needs a Presence or Smarts roll against DS 10, 12 or 14. The party may use any Standing, contact or ally to help.

Tier Who Needs Exposure DS
1: Local Port officials, enforcers, frontier governors 3 10
2: Sector Governors, Senate aides, trade guild heads 6 12
3: Inner circle Senators, Chancellor’s staff, the cartel’s backer 9 14

Success removes that tier’s power and lowers Exposure by 3 as the evidence is spent. Failure can cost the party Standing, Evidence or friends.

Standing

Track Standing from 0 to 5 with each major group, as in Order Standing in ERA-OR and Syndicate Standing in FT-SCUM. The groups are the Republic (officials and courts), the Jedi Order, each syndicate and each clan.

Standing Result
0 The group treats you as a threat.
1-2 Tolerated. Work and help at normal prices.
3-4 Trusted. +3 to rolls to persuade members and use their facilities.
5 Inner circle. Safe houses, shared intelligence and one favour a session.

Divided loyalties. Standing with the Republic and Standing with a syndicate or clan do not conflict until you act. If you are caught working for both, lose 1 Standing with each.

Clans

Mandalorian clans are honour-bound warrior families. They sell their skill to people who respect their code and may refuse those who do not. A character with a clan background starts at Clan Standing 3 with their own clan and 0 with others.

  • The Code. Clan members will not betray a contract they have accepted, abandon a clan member in battle, or attack the defenceless. Breaking the code costs the character 2 Clan Standing with their clan, and they may be hunted.

  • Clan work. A clan’s contracts pay as in FT-SCUM and a clan may lend gear, warriors or a safe place to a party that has Standing 3 or more.

Clan gear

All clan gear uses Core profiles. The special rules below are optional and untested.

Item Profile Special rule
Clan plate Full Armour (Heavy 2) Slashing critical hits do not reduce its durability
Light clan armour Reinforced Armour (Light 2) Comlink and visor built into the helmet
Jetpack Medium item Once per combat, use your Movement to ignore Opportunity Actions and move up to 1 zone vertically
Wrist gauntlet Light Shield Holds a Light Ranged weapon (Hidden) and a grapple cable

Era backgrounds

Republic Fixer

You are an aide, auditor, investigator or negotiator. You know how favours travel in a government, and who owes whom. You may be honest or compromised, and you probably know which.

  • Trained in: Fixer tools, light weapons, cloth and non-metal armour

  • Fixer tools: anything used for records, administration, bribery, negotiation and other legal or political acts

  • Equipment: Heavy, datapad, stylus, official credentials, forgery kit, comlink, 1 piece of Evidence (value 1), 1 contact (FT-SCUM level 1 if on)

  • Start with Standing 1 with the Republic.

Clan Warrior

You were raised in a warrior clan and trained to fight, to hunt and to keep the code. Your clan is your family and your armour is your history.

  • Trained in: Clan tools, all weapons, all armour, jetpacks, grenades

  • Clan tools: anything used for tracking, field repair, armour care, survival and other martial acts

  • Equipment: Medium, clan insignia, repair kit, 5 glow rods, comlink, ration pack, 1 smoke grenade, your family’s story

  • Start with Clan Standing 3 with your clan.

Backgrounds from SW-BASE work as written. The Jedi Padawan background from ERA-OR may be used with the GM’s permission (use the Hidden Force preset). The Sith Acolyte background is for a hidden villain or a campaign twist.

Factions

Faction Wants Usually allied with Usually opposed to
Galactic Senate Stability, trade and its own survival Trade guilds, Core governors Anyone who exposes it
Republic courts and agents Law and order Jedi Order Syndicates, corrupt officials
Jedi Order Peace and the balance of the Force Honest Republic officials Dark-side agents
Hutt kajidics Profit, territory and respect Whoever pays Anyone who threatens trade
Mandalorian clans Honour, contracts and a place in the galaxy Their paymasters Anyone who breaks a contract
Outer Rim syndicates Profit and control of trade routes Corrupt officials Rivals, honest officials
The conspiracy Power and secrecy Anyone it can buy The party, the press, the Jedi
Hidden Sith To remain unseen until the time is right The conspiracy, quietly The Jedi Order

The conspiracy

Give the conspiracy a face at each tier. For each, note a leader, a goal, a resource and a weakness, as for a syndicate in FT-SCUM.

Tier Leader Goal Resource Weakness
1: Local Port Administrator Orlen Vask Keep the Hutt’s cargo moving and take a cut Customs officers and a police chief Is in debt to a rival syndicate
2: Sector Governor Isaura Thenn Win a Senate seat Money, a private fleet (Corvette) Is afraid of her own bodyguard captain
3: Inner circle A senator no one suspects Control the trade lanes and the vote on a new law Spies, blackmail, a Sith patron Needs the other two tiers to stay hidden

Change these as you like. The tiers do not need to be one chain: they may be three separate threats that the party finds they share a payer.

Starting on the Outer Rim

A good opening for this Era is a job from a Hutt on an Outer Rim world.

  1. The contract. Use the FT-SCUM contract board. The Hutt’s agent states a quarry, a client, a reward and terms. Use the Catch to plant a seed of corruption: the cargo has a Republic seal, the quarry is a clerk, or the client is a Republic official.

  2. The party’s position. Each character starts with 100 credits, Hutt Standing 1 and 1 Local Heat on the world. Exposure is 0.

  3. The first Evidence. Let the party find one piece of Evidence (value 1) by the end of the first job. A second piece (value 2) should be dangerous to get.

  4. The first hook. The Hutt’s job and the conspiracy should cross. The quarry may be a witness. The cargo may be a bribe. The Hutt may be a victim, or a partner, of the people the party is chasing.

  5. Escalation. At Exposure 3, a Core-world contact warns the party that someone is asking about them. At Exposure 6, the Hutt’s price goes up, or his loyalty goes down.

Suggested stack. SW-BASE, ERA-RT, FT-SCUM, FT-SHIPS and FT-FORCE (Hidden Force). Add FT-DROIDS if a player wants a droid. Use APP-LOOT for rewards and the Military and Officer’s tables for evidence caches.

ERA-CW: Clone Wars

Module card
Code ERA-CW
Requires SW-BASE
Pairs well with FT-FORCE, FT-DROIDS, FT-SHIPS
Conflicts ERA-OR, ERA-RT, ERA-GE
Overrides Which enemies are common and how large fights are run
Adds War Effort track, Squad Orders, Droid Squads, two backgrounds, factions

The Republic and the Separatists are at war across hundreds of worlds. Armies of clone troopers and battle droids clash under Jedi generals, while ordinary people try to survive between the lines. This is a game of squads, sieges and impossible odds.

Era rules

War Effort. The GM tracks a campaign-wide War Effort from 0 to 10, starting at 5. Each successful mission for your side adds 1, and each failure subtracts 1. At 10, your side gets a campaign-changing advantage such as reinforcements or a captured base. At 0, your side suffers a major reversal. Both sides track their own score if you play a split-faction table.

Squad Orders. Once per combat, a character with Soldier tools or Jedi tools may use their Interaction to give one ally in their zone Advantage on that ally’s next roll.

Droid Squads. To keep large fights fast, a group of up to 6 identical droids acts as one combatant called a squad.

  • The squad has the HP of one droid and loses one droid each time it takes damage equal to that HP.

  • The squad’s attack deals +1 damage for every 2 droids still standing, to a maximum of +3.

  • Area attacks such as grenades remove 2 droids at once.

Droid HP CD AR Weapon Damage
Battle droid 5 11 0 Blaster rifle 5
Commando droid 8 13 1 Blaster carbine 5
Super battle droid 12 10 3 Wrist blaster 6
Droideka 15 12 5 Twin repeaters 7

Armour and weapons. Blaster rifles and heavy trooper armour are standard issue. Vibroweapons are rare except among Jedi and elite guards.

Era backgrounds

Clone Trooper

You were grown and trained for war and you know your squad better than any family. You are loyal to the Republic and to your brothers, and you have never known a life outside the army.

  • Trained in: Soldier tools, all weapons, all armour

  • Start with: Cover Fire trait, Rank 1 in Health ability

  • Languages: Basic only

  • Equipment: Heavy, 5 glow rods, unit rank insignia, repair kit, extra carry pouches (+10), comlink, squad colours, blaster rifle and trooper kit

  • You use Human species stats except Adaptable, which is replaced by Squad Orders at no cost.

Separatist Operative

You work for the Confederacy as an agent, saboteur or mercenary officer. You know how to talk to droid commanders, trade bosses and desperate worlds.

  • Trained in: Operative tools, light weapons, armour with Bulk 0 to 2

  • Operative tools: anything used for sabotage, slicing, infiltration and negotiating with droid forces

  • Equipment: Heavy, 5 glow rods, slicer kit, forgery kit, disguise kit, comlink, 1 smoke grenade, droid command code chip

Species notes

The twelve core species are common. From More Species, Geonosians, Neimoidians, Muun, Kaminoans and Gungans are closely tied to the war, and the GM decides which of the rest are present. Clone troopers use Human stats. Droids and droid armies are in FT-DROIDS, and the Droid Squad rules above work with or without that module.

Factions

Faction Wants Usually allied with Usually opposed to
Galactic Republic Victory and unity Jedi Order, clone army Separatists
Separatist Confederacy Independence and profit Trade guilds, droid armies Republic
Jedi Order Peace and survival Republic Dark-side agents
Hutt cartels Profit and influence Whoever pays Anyone blocking trade
Mercenary guilds Credits The highest bidder Anyone who breaks a contract

ERA-GE: Galactic Empire

Module card
Code ERA-GE
Requires SW-BASE
Pairs well with FT-SCUM, FT-SHIPS, FT-DROIDS
Conflicts ERA-OR, ERA-RT, ERA-CW
Overrides How Force use, crime and travel are treated by authority
Adds Heat track, Checkpoints, two backgrounds, factions

The Empire rules the galaxy through fear, garrison fleets and propaganda. Resistance is risky, the Force is rare and hunted, and a smuggler can be a hero or a traitor depending on who is counting. This is a game of small acts that add up.

Era rules

Heat. Heat is a track from 0 to 10 that measures how much attention Imperial authorities pay to your group. The GM adds 1 Heat whenever you openly resist the Empire, such as shooting troopers, hacking Imperial networks or helping a wanted person. Heat is separate from Fame and Infamy, which measure public reputation.

Heat What happens
0-2 Normal life. Occasional checkpoints.
3-5 Patrols look for you. DS of Checkpoints increases.
6-8 Inquisitorial agents and fast interceptors are sent after you.
9-10 You are a named enemy of the Empire with a bounty on every head.

Heat falls by 1 each time you spend a full rest at a safe house without being traced, or when you complete a mission that clears your name.

Checkpoints. To pass an Imperial checkpoint, roll Presence or Smarts against DS 10 + Heat. A forgery kit gives +3 and a fake credential chip gives Advantage.

Force use. If FT-FORCE is on, using a Force power in public adds 1 Heat. Wielding a lightsaber openly adds 2 Heat.

Human-first option. A GM who wants a harsher tone can give non-human characters Disadvantage on Presence rolls to officials in Core Worlds.

Armour and weapons. Blaster rifles and trooper armour are standard for Imperial forces. Rebels and smugglers favour carbines, pistols and anything they can modify.

Era backgrounds

Rebel Operative

You joined the resistance as a scout, spy, pilot or saboteur. You work in cells, with few resources and fewer friends, and your cause matters more to you than your life.

  • Trained in: Operative tools, light weapons, armour with Bulk 0 to 2

  • Operative tools: anything used for sabotage, slicing, infiltration, coded comms and field repair

  • Equipment: Heavy, 5 glow rods, slicer kit, forgery kit, disguise kit, comlink, 1 smoke grenade, cell contact list

Imperial Officer

You served or still serve the Empire in an academy, garrison or bureau. You know its ranks, forms and codes, and you may be a true believer or a quiet defector.

  • Trained in: Imperial tools, light weapons, all armour with Bulk 0 to 2

  • Imperial tools: anything used for command, logistics, paperwork, interrogation and other bureaucratic or military acts

  • Equipment: Medium, datapad, stylus, rank insignia, official credentials, comlink, standard-issue sidearm, galaxy map

Species notes

The twelve core species are available, and the GM decides which of the More Species are present. Under the Human-first option, Wookiees and other species face open prejudice and slavery in Imperial space. This can drive a story but should be agreed with the table first.

Factions

Faction Wants Usually allied with Usually opposed to
Galactic Empire Total control and order Garrison fleets, loyal governors Rebel Alliance, crime syndicates that refuse tribute
Rebel Alliance Freedom and a restored Republic Free traders, defecting officers Galactic Empire
Crime syndicates Profit and territory Whoever pays Rivals, the Empire when it interferes
Bounty hunters’ guild Contracts Highest bidder Anyone who breaks a contract
Free traders Safe passage Everyone, quietly Pirates, tax collectors

FT-FORCE: The Force

Module card
Code FT-FORCE
Requires SW-BASE
Pairs well with ERA-OR, ERA-CW, ERA-RT
Conflicts None. In ERA-GE and ERA-RT the Force is rare.
Overrides Replaces Fantasy Theme Magic. Angelic becomes Light, Demonic becomes Dark, magic points become Force Points.
Adds Force Pool, alignment, 31 powers, lightsabers, forms, kyber attunement, Corruption

The Force follows the same structure as Fantasy Theme Magic so that every Ability Database spell converts one-to-one. Costs, ranges, damage and scaling below are the Ability Database values, re-flavoured for the setting.

In this module: Force Pool and powers · Alignment · Corruption · Optional rule: Tiered Force · Light powers (Senses) · Dark powers (Presence) · Neutral powers (any alignment) · Lightsabers · Kyber crystals and attunement · Lightsaber forms

Force Pool and powers

When you choose the Force you start with 100 Force Points and 2 powers at level 1. Either power may be swapped for a weapon instead. Every level after that you may choose 1 Force power or a trait.

  • Force Points go up by 50 every level.

  • Force DS = 10 + your Senses or Presence Rank.

  • Force Attack ability = Senses or Presence dice.

  • Light powers use Senses. Dark powers use Presence. Neutral powers use either.

  • Using a power is a Main Action unless stated, and costs the amount shown at the level you use it.

  • A light rest returns Force Points equal to your Senses or Presence score x 10, as in the Core System.

The GM decides who is Force-sensitive in the campaign. A table that wants the Force to be common lets anyone take it. A table that wants it to be rare lets only characters from Jedi, Sith or Mystic backgrounds take it, or uses the Hidden Force preset.

Alignment

Every Force user tracks alignment on a seven-box meter: 3 Light, 1 Neutral in the middle and 3 Dark.

Light Light Light Neutral Dark Dark Dark
1 2 3 0 1 2 3

  • A character with 2 points on either side cannot use powers of the opposing side.

  • Neutral powers can be used by any alignment.

  • Reaching the maximum on either side gives +5 to all rolls with powers of that alignment and +2 to Force DS.

  • The GM awards a point when you use a power in a way that fits its side, such as healing and protecting for Light, or killing and dominating for Dark. A point is removed by an atonement scene or a long period of acting against your alignment.

Corruption

A character at full Dark is Consumed. Once per session, the GM may ask for a Smarts roll against DS 12 to resist a dark impulse. On a fail, you act on it. This is the Force equivalent of the Fantasy Theme’s Vampirism and Lycanthropy warnings.

Optional rule: Tiered Force

If you want the Force without a point pool, use the Superpowers rules in the Ability Database instead. Telekinesis (SP-026), Telepathy (SP-027), Super Speed (SP-023), Enhanced Senses (SP-006) and others fit the Force well. They have no point cost, start at Tier 1 and go up each time you take them at a level up, and Tiers 4 and 5 cost 1 and 2 Fatigue. Light and Dark alignment still applies.

Light powers (Senses)

Power Database Range Cost Effect
Force Absorb AS-001 Self 15 + 10 every 3 levels Opportunity Action. Absorb 5 damage from an attack that hits you. The amount rises by 5 every 3 levels.
Force Heal AS-002 Self or touch None Heal from a pool equal to your Senses Rank x2. The pool grows at levels 3 and 6 as the database lists. A light rest refills it.
Force Mend AS-003 Self or touch None listed Full Action. Heal 1 wound, +1 every 3 levels.
Improved Heal AS-004 1 zone or self None Needs Force Heal. Your heal reaches up to 2 allies in your zone and cures poisons and venom.
Mind Trick AS-006 1 zone 5 + 5 every level A humanoid makes a Smarts roll or is charmed for the duration. On a success it knows you tried. In combat it gets +10 to its roll.
Force Illusion AS-007 (Projection) 2 zones 20 + 20 every level Create an illusory image or sound as a distraction. Seeing through it needs a Smarts roll. +1 illusion every 3 levels.
Force Barrier AS-008 (Protection Sphere) Self or none 100 + 50 every 2 levels 1 minute. A bubble around creatures equal to your Senses Rank blocks projectiles and heals those inside by 2 each turn. You cannot move while it lasts.
Force Ghost (GM option) AS-005 (Intangibility) Self 20 + 20 every level 1 minute or 3 turns. You are incorporeal and can neither deal nor take damage. +1 turn every level.

Dark powers (Presence)

Power Database Range Cost Effect
Dark Lash DS-001 (Blood Spear) 2 zones, straight line None On a hit, deal 4 damage.
Force Choke DS-002 1 zone 50 + 35 every level Health roll: 12 damage on a fail, half on a pass. +4 damage every 3 levels.
Force Drain DS-003 (Drain Life) 2 zones 25 + 10 every level On a hit, deal damage equal to the enemy’s Level and heal half of it (round up). +2 every level.
Force Burst DS-004 (Fireball) 2 zones 50 + 35 every 3 levels Agility roll: 12 damage on a fail, half on a pass. +4 damage every 3 levels.
Force Lightning DS-005 2 zones, straight line 50 + 50 every level Each creature in the path rolls Agility: 9 lightning damage on a fail, half on a pass. +3 damage every 3 levels.
Sith Blight DS-006 (Poison Seed) Touch 200 A 24-hour ritual makes a seed. Placed in a creature, it rolls Health or takes 12 poison damage each turn until it passes.
Mind Probe DS-007 (Probe Mind) 2 zones 50 + 50 every level Smarts roll or you learn 1 secret. +1 secret every level.
Force Scream DS-008 (Screech) 2 zones 35 + 35 every 3 levels Health roll: half damage on a pass, 5 damage and Deafened on a fail. +5 damage every 3 levels.
Force Sight DS-009 (Seer Sight) 2 zones 50 See through a creature’s eyes. Afterwards it makes a Senses roll to notice.
Force Shroud DS-010 (Shadow Sphere) 1 zone 20 A sphere of shadows around you. Creatures in range are Blinded unless they have night vision or light.

Neutral powers (any alignment)

Power Database Range Cost Effect
Battle Meditation US-001 Self or touch 100 Opportunity Action. Change the outcome of a roll to a Critical Success. 1 minute or 3 turns.
Force Push US-003 (Blast) 2 zones 10 On a hit, deal 6 damage. +6 at 5th level.
Beast Bond US-004 (Control Animal) 1 zone 80 A beast makes a Smarts roll or is charmed. In combat it always passes.
Droid Crush US-006 (Crush Construct) 2 zones 5 + 5 every level Deal 4 damage to a droid or construct. +4 every level.
Force Strength US-007 (Enhance Brawn) Self Brawn Rank x 10 per turn +5 to all Brawn rolls.
Force Endurance US-008 (Enhance Health) Self 50 per turn +5 to all Health rolls and HP x2.
Force Speed US-009 (Enhance Speed) Self 50 per turn +5 to all Agility rolls and +1 zone of movement.
Force Jump US-010 (Jump) Self or touch 50 Jump height increases by your height. 1 minute or 3 turns.
Force Leap US-011 (Leap) Self or touch 50 Jump distance becomes half a zone. 1 minute or 3 turns.
Force Hurl US-013 (Orb) 2 zones 10 Gather objects into an orb. On a hit, deal 5 damage. +5 at level 4.
Force Cloak US-015 (Stealth) Self or touch 20 + 20 every level You are hidden. Detecting you needs a Senses roll at Disadvantage. Duration +1 every level.
Force Stun US-016 (Stun) 2 zones 50 + 50 every level Health roll or the target is paralysed for the duration. +1 target every level.
Telekinesis US-018 2 zones 10 Move an object or creature. A resisting creature rolls Brawn. If it collides, all involved take 5 damage.

The Fantasy Theme powers Beastshift, Create Structure, Manifest Object, Shapeshift and Summon are not converted, but the GM may allow them as Force techniques.

Lightsabers

A lightsaber uses the Ignore weapon tag, so its damage bypasses Armour Rating and goes straight to HP. You must be trained in lightsabers to wield one: take Jedi Padawan or Sith Acolyte as a background, or spend a trait pick. An untrained wielder has Disadvantage on attacks, and on a critical failure takes 3 damage.

Weapon Profile Unique tags Range Damage
Shoto sabre Light Melee Ignore 4
Lightsaber Medium Melee Ignore 6
Double-bladed sabre Heavy Melee Ignore, Reach 3 (Attack Twice)

The Strider trait lets a character with a lightsaber knock aside blaster bolts as described in SW-BASE.

Kyber crystals and attunement

A lightsaber needs a kyber crystal. Light-side crystals are blue or green, Dark-side crystals are red, and Neutral crystals are white or yellow. Building one is a 24-hour Master crafting task (DS 10) and needs a crystal.

To attune a crystal you spend the Force Points of the power you are binding, then roll your Force Attack dice against the DS. The result sets the level. Choose damage or utility. You may take both only at the lower-level values, and you still make the same DS roll.

Level DS Damage Utility Charges
Novice 8 2 1 4
Adept 9 3 2 3
Master 11 4 3 2

Charges are the number of combats the attunement lasts before it fades. A middle rest spent meditating with the crystal restores all of them.

Lightsaber forms

When wielding a lightsaber you can use forms that sharpen your skill. A form is chosen in place of a trait and must be activated as an Interaction. It stays until you switch to another. Only one form may be active at a time. Form numbers match the Fantasy Theme’s Sword Forms and the names are flavour.

Form Name Description
1 Shii-Cho Advantage on attacks when used against other swords.
2 Makashi When you are targeted by an attack, add +1 to Combat Defence. If the attack misses, you gain an Opportunity Action against the attacker with your sabre only.
3 Juyo You naturally deal critical damage on a hit, but every 2 rounds you suffer 1 Fatigue point.
4 Soresu Needs a lightsaber. Ranged attacks have Disadvantage against you. If one hits, you may deflect it back if you roll higher than the attacker’s hit roll.
5 Ataru When an attack misses you, you may make a non-damaging attack that Stuns the enemy for the rest of the round.
6 Niman When attacked with a Dark power, your next hit is a critical. If you deal over 15 damage with it, add 2 Dark points to your alignment.
7 Djem So When attacked with a Force power you take the damage, but the Force Points used for it are added to your Force Points. If none were used, gain 10.

Martial Arts styles from the Fantasy Theme are unchanged and can be used in any Star Wars campaign.

FT-SHIPS: Starships and Vehicles

Module card
Code FT-SHIPS
Requires SW-BASE
Pairs well with Any Era
Conflicts None
Overrides Nothing. Extends the Core System Ships table.
Adds Starship classes, crew roles, hyperspace rolls, scale rule, ground vehicles

The Core System’s Ships chapter already covers starships in space or vessels on the sea, and gives stats you can use as a template. This module reskins that table for the galaxy and adds crew roles, jumps and ground vehicles.

In this module: Starships and Crews · Travel and Navigation · Vehicle Encounters

Starships and Crews

Ship classes

Values are taken from the Core System Ships table.

Ship Crew HP CD Armour Weapon Attack Damage Zones
Starfighter 1 8 16 0 Laser cannon 1d12 2 Small
Light freighter Up to 10 15 13 7/7 Turret 1d12 8 Medium
Corvette Up to 30 25 11 14/14 Heavy cannon 1d12 16 Large
Cruiser Up to 50 50 8 25/25 Turbolaser 1d12 30 2 per move

Crew roles

Role Roll Helpful traits
Pilot Agility or Smarts. Moves the ship and sets its CD. Heart Of Oak, Hot Pilot
Gunner Senses or Agility. Makes attacks with the ship’s weapon. Weapon Mastery
Engineer Smarts. Repairs the ship mid-fight. Handyman, Jury-Rigger
Sensors and comms Senses or Smarts. Scans, jams and hails. Slicer

A ship takes its turn on the crew’s initiative. Each crew member may use their Main Action for their role.

Travel and Navigation

Hyperspace

To jump, the pilot or navigator makes a Smarts roll. Spacer tools add +3. On a critical failure the ship arrives off-course or damaged, as the GM decides.

Route DS
Charted lane 8
Rarely used route 10
Uncharted or hostile space 12
Emergency jump with no plot 14

Vehicle Encounters

Scale rule

An attack from a smaller ship deals half damage (rounded down) to a ship of the next size class up or larger. Personal weapons deal 1 damage to ships unless they use the Siege profile.

Ground vehicles

Vehicle Crew HP CD Armour Speed
Speeder bike 1 5 14 0 3 zones
Landspeeder Up to 4 8 12 2 2 zones
Armoured transport Up to 10 20 9 10 2 zones
Scout walker 2 15 11 5/5 1 zone, chin cannon 8 damage

Boarding

When a ship is boarded, switch to character-scale zones. The ship’s interior counts as a map and its HP counts as structure HP for scenery.

FT-DROIDS: Droids and Companions

Module card
Code FT-DROIDS
Requires SW-BASE
Pairs well with ERA-CW, FT-SHIPS, FT-SCUM
Conflicts None
Overrides Species (a player may choose a droid frame instead). Rests for droids. Healing for droids.
Adds Six droid frames, five droid traits (SW-008 to SW-012), droid rules, droid and creature companions, upgrades, restraining bolts

Droids are mechanics, translators, surgeons, soldiers, pilots and sometimes friends. Some are little more than machines following orders, and others grow personalities and loyalties that make them hard to tell from the people they serve.

Droids use the same attributes, traits, levels and equipment rules as everyone else. This module only adds what makes a droid different.

In this module: Creating and Playing a Droid · Maintenance and Upgrades · Droids and Companions in Play

Creating and Playing a Droid

Playing a droid

Build a droid character the normal way: 10 ranks, a background, 2 weapons or a power, 1 armour and traits. The only change is that you pick a droid frame in place of a species. A droid has no species traits and no Basic-speaking bonus, so check the languages line of your frame.

Backgrounds work as written. Swap any rations, incense or other organic items in your background’s equipment for 3 power cells.

Droid frames

Each frame lists its stats in the same format as a species.

Astromech

Stats

  • Start with Handyman trait

  • Rank 1 in Smarts ability

  • Utility arms: you have a built-in repair kit that counts as Technician tools and cannot be disarmed

  • Languages: Binary, understands Basic but cannot speak it

  • Size: Small

  • Limitation: no hands. You cannot use weapons or gear made for hands without a Weapon Mount or Tool Attachment upgrade.

Astromechs are squat, tireless repair units that live in the guts of ships. They are fearless about danger to themselves and bad-tempered about danger to their crew.

Protocol

Stats

  • Start with Gift Of The Gab trait

  • Rank 1 in Presence ability

  • Translator: after one scene of hearing a language, you can speak and understand it

  • Languages: Basic, 4 extra

  • Size: Medium

  • Limitation: programmed caution. Disadvantage on rolls to resist Frightened.

Protocol droids are built for etiquette, translation and negotiation, and they are almost always smarter than they let on.

Medical

Stats

  • Start with Mortar & Pestle trait

  • Rank 1 in Smarts ability

  • Diagnostic scanner: Advantage on Smarts rolls to identify injuries, poisons and diseases. Counts as trained in medical tools and as having the Field Medic trait (SW-007).

  • Languages: Basic, 1 extra

  • Size: Medium

  • Limitation: oath of care. You cannot make an attack roll against a creature that has not attacked you or an ally this combat.

Medical droids are patient, precise and unafraid of blood, and the best ones have saved more lives than any general.

Security

Stats

  • Start with Reinforced Chassis trait (SW-009), Rank 1

  • Rank 1 in Brawn or Agility ability, your choice

  • Built-in blaster port: a Light Ranged weapon (Hidden, Reload, 1 zone, 3 damage) that cannot be disarmed

  • Languages: Basic

  • Size: Medium

  • Limitation: conspicuous. Disadvantage on Stealth and disguise rolls, and armed droids are banned in many places.

Security droids guard vaults, patrol corridors and escort officials. They are tough and built to be seen.

Scout

Stats

  • Start with Heart Of Oak trait

  • Rank 1 in Senses ability

  • Sensor suite: you can see through any type of darkness and have Advantage on Senses rolls to spot hidden things

  • Languages: Basic, Binary

  • Size: Medium

  • Limitation: light chassis. Your carry weight is Brawn Rank x 2 instead of x 5.

Scout droids chart lanes, sweep ruins and ride ahead of the crew. They are fast, observant and rarely sent anywhere safe.

Labour

Stats

  • Start with Shoulders Of Giants trait

  • Rank 1 in Brawn ability

  • Heavy servos: Advantage on Brawn rolls to lift, push, pull or break things

  • Languages: Basic

  • Size: Large

  • Limitation: clumsy hands. Disadvantage on delicate Agility rolls, such as picking pockets or fine repairs, unless you have Integrated Tools.

Labour droids haul cargo, run cranes and demolish things. They are strong, uncomplaining and easy to underestimate.

Custom frame

Agree a frame with your GM: Rank 1 in 1 ability, 1 trait, 1 useful feature, 1 limitation of equal weight, languages and a size. A limitation should cost the character something at the table at least once a session.

Droid rules

  • Mechanical. Droids are immune to poison, disease and gas effects. They cannot be Charmed by Mind Trick, which only works on humanoids.

  • Vulnerable to ion. Droids are vulnerable to ion attacks as in the Core System vulnerability rule: ion damage is critical damage, and the DS of any ion Stun effect against a droid is raised by 2.

  • Recharge. A droid does not sleep. It must spend 4 hours a day plugged into power, or it gains 1 Fatigue for every day it goes without. A recharge counts as a middle rest for droid characters.

  • Rests. A droid gets the benefits of a light rest by cooling down for 1 hour. Its magic or Force Points, if any, return as normal.

  • Healing. A droid cannot use bacta, medpacs or stims. It heals through repairs (below). Antitoxins have no effect.

  • Wounds. Droids take Wounds as normal. A droid’s Wounds are removed by workshop repairs only.

  • Zero HP. A droid at 0 HP is shut down and makes Death Rolls as normal. On a failure it is destroyed. A destroyed droid can be rebuilt if its memory core survives.

  • Droid Crush (Force power) deals its damage to a droid as normal. A droid has no Force sensitivity unless the GM allows it.

Droid traits
