Statistics

Solo HP Group HP Combat Defence Armour Atk Dmg/Hit Wind-Up
17 68 15 3 +10 12 24

Special Abilities. None listed. The standard enemy rules in the opening guide apply.

Armour-Breaker
Level 5
Theme Fantasy (also Modern: Breaching Marksman)
Tags Piercer (Anti-Armour)

Description. A duellist with a war-pick built to punch through plate.

Statistics

Solo HP Group HP Combat Defence Armour Atk Dmg/Hit Wind-Up
15 60 15 2 +10 14 28

Special Abilities

  • Armour Rending – This creature’s attacks ignore the target’s Armour Rating entirely. The Damage Floor is irrelevant against it – full damage always lands.
  • Exploit the Gap – Once per encounter, a hit against a target with Armour Rating 3 or higher deals +4 additional damage.
  • Sunder – As an Interaction, attack a target’s armour instead of the target. On a hit, that target’s Armour Rating is reduced by 1 for the rest of the encounter, minimum 0. A character can repair this with the Handyman trait and a Repair Kit between fights.

Encounter Advice. Does not out-tank a heavily armoured player – it goes around the armour instead. Best fielded against a party with a stacked-AR tank; noticeably worse value against a lightly armoured or magic-focused party, since its whole kit is spent bypassing a defence that is not there. Under the Damage Floor its edge is smaller than it was in v1.2 but far more consistent.

Spiked Brute
Level 5
Theme Fantasy (also Modern: Powered Bodyguard)
Tags Retaliator (Reflect / Counter-Damage)

Description. Barbed iron plating that punishes every blow.

Statistics

Solo HP Group HP Combat Defence Armour Atk Dmg/Hit Wind-Up
17 68 13 4 +10 12 24

Special Abilities

  • Thorned Hide – Whenever a creature hits this creature with a melee attack, the attacker takes damage equal to this creature’s Armour Rating. Armour and the Damage Floor apply to this damage as normal.
  • Retaliate – Once per round, when hit by a melee attack, this creature may immediately make one basic attack against the attacker as a free action. It cannot Retaliate on the turn it resolves a Wind-Up.
  • Spiked Bulwark – For the purpose of Thorned Hide only, treat this creature’s Armour Rating as 1 higher.

Encounter Advice. Invites melee instead of avoiding it, and makes attackers regret closing in. The direct answer to a party that just wades in and swings – the harder they hit it, the more it hits back. Weak against a party that leans on ranged attacks or spells, since its whole kit only triggers off being struck in melee.

Inquisitor
Level 6
Theme Fantasy (also Modern: Suppressor)
Tags Warden (Anti-Magic)

Description. A mage-hunter warded against sorcery.

Statistics

Solo HP Group HP Combat Defence Armour Atk Dmg/Hit Wind-Up
26 104 13 4 +10 13 26

Special Abilities

  • Spellward Hide – Halve all damage taken from Universal, Angelic and Demonic Spells, after Armour Rating and the Damage Floor are applied. Superpowers are not affected – a Warden is no answer to a Voryn.
  • Closing Charge – Once per round, as a free action after moving, this creature may move one additional zone towards the nearest spellcaster it can see.
  • Mana Sever – On a hit, the target’s next spell cast this encounter costs double. This does not stack with itself.

Encounter Advice. Built to shut down spellcasters: resistant to magic damage and actively closing distance on anyone slinging spells from range. Weak against a party leaning entirely on mundane weapons – its statline assumes it will be eating spell damage, so a purely martial party gets none of that discount.

Berserker Charger
Level 6
Theme Fantasy (also Modern: Blitz Breacher)
Tags Breacher (Anti-Ranged)

Description. Closes ground faster than arrows can track.

Statistics

Solo HP Group HP Combat Defence Armour Atk Dmg/Hit Wind-Up
26 104 14 3 +10 14 28

Special Abilities

  • Weaving Advance – While moving towards a target, ranged attacks against this creature have Disadvantage.
  • Grounded – On a hit against a creature using a ranged weapon, that target cannot move away from this creature until the start of its next turn. A target may spend its Main action to break free with a contested Brawn or Agility roll.
  • Bull Rush – When moving, this creature may pass directly through zones containing enemies without provoking, so long as it ends its movement adjacent to a ranged attacker.

Encounter Advice. Its whole job is closing the gap on anyone shooting or casting from a distance and pinning them in melee where their ranged kit stops mattering. Weak against a party that fights entirely in melee already – there is no distance for it to close, so its core trick does nothing.

Battle Cleric
Level 6
Theme Fantasy (also Modern: Combat Medic)
Tags Warpriest (Healer / Support)

Description. Mends the warband’s wounds mid-fight.

Statistics

Solo HP Group HP Combat Defence Armour Atk Dmg/Hit Wind-Up
27 109 13 5 +10 8 16

Special Abilities

  • Mending Word – Once per round, as a Main action, heal one ally within range for one-eighth of that ally’s maximum Hit Points, rounded up. It cannot heal itself with this. (Scaling the heal to the ally rather than to its own damage keeps a Warpriest worth killing against a Group statline without letting it out-heal a Solo.)
  • Ward – Once per round, as an Interaction, grant one ally a shield equal to this creature’s Dmg/Hit; the next hit against them is reduced by that amount before Health is lost.
  • Mass Mending – Once per encounter, instead of Mending Word, heal every ally in the zone for twice this creature’s Dmg/Hit.

Encounter Advice. Deals the least damage of any specialist here – its job is keeping other enemies standing, not hitting hard itself. It forces the party into a real target-priority decision: burn this down first, or let it keep undoing your damage on everything else. Weak whenever a party can burst it down before it gets more than a use or two out of its healing.

Cult Enchanter

Solo HP Group HP Combat Defence Armour Atk Dmg/Hit Wind-Up
33 132 13 4 +11 11 22

Special Abilities

  • Overwhelm the Mind – On a failed resist against any of this creature’s effects, the target also has Disadvantage on its next roll.
  • Puppet Strings – As a Main action, force a Charmed creature to make a basic attack against an ally of its choice. A character forced to attack an ally at 0 Hit Points deals damage but does not inflict the 2 Wounds from the Downed rule.

33 132 13 4 +11 6 12

Appendix: Version History and Designer’s Notes

What Changed From v2.0

v2.0 got the shape of a fight right (short, sharp, telegraphed) but its numbers had never been measured. Tested against a realistic party, its HP was too high, its Standard fights ran long, and its Hard and Deadly rows were not far apart in danger. Four things changed.

  • Hit Points are down 20–40% – Group HP now sits where a reference party clears a Standard fight in about four rounds. Level 5 drops from 116 to 68 Group HP; Level 7 from 180 to 132. Damage is unchanged, so every enemy is exactly as dangerous per hit and simply stays on the table for less time.

  • Enemies now have an attack bonus – Every enemy attacks with 1d10 + Atk against the player’s Combat Defence, and the bonus is a single flat number per Level (+8 at Level 1 up to +11 at Level 7). Before, the Compendium gave Dmg/Hit but left the to-hit side to the GM, which made two tables run the same stat block very differently. See the Atk rule under the Scaling Rule.

  • The Threat Budget is re-measured – Routine, Standard, Hard and Deadly are redefined from simulation, each with its measured Wounds and chance of a character dropping to 0. A new Boss row uses the Boss Action so a Group statline is a real set-piece rather than a speed bump. The old Level 8 Group row and the Warpriest/Summoner Deadly row are gone.

  • Specialists and recipes are re-priced – All ten specialists were slotted into a Standard fight in place of one generalist. Each now lands within roughly 25% of a generalist’s Wounds. The Warpriest heals a fraction of its ally’s HP instead of a multiple of its own damage, Spiked Bulwark is +1, the Blightcaller’s venom is lighter, and the Building More recipes are updated to match. The Damage Floor and the Armour Rating cap of 8 are now in the Core System.

Designer’s Notes

Commentary, not rules text.

The maths behind the numbers

Player Hit Points come from Health rank × 5, so 5 / 10 / 15 / 20, or 9 / 16 / 23 / 32 plus one per level with the Healthy trait. The realistic band for a character who has spent sensibly is 10–15 Hit Points at low level and 20–32 at high level, plus three Wounds each of which negates a hit outright.

Dmg/Hit is set so that an unarmoured character at the matching level takes roughly a third to a half of their Hit Points per hit – three hits to drop, or six with Wounds spent. A fully armoured character at the same level takes roughly half that after the Damage Floor, so five to six hits, or eight to nine with Wounds. That is the intended spread: armour buys you time, not immunity.

Group HP is set so that the reference party clears a Standard fight (four Solo statlines at party level) in about four rounds. v2.0’s Level 5 at 116 Group HP and Level 7 at 180 were tuned by feel; against the simulated party they ran long. Level 7 now sits at 132. Solo HP is Group HP ÷ 4.

Combat Defence is trimmed at Levels 2 and 4 and capped at 15 for every enemy and every recipe: a Rank 4 attacker averages 12 on 1d10 + 1d12, so CD 16 or above pushes a fully invested character below a one-in-three hit rate before criticals, which makes the fight feel arbitrary rather than hard.

The reference party. Four characters, each at the Compendium’s level, moderately optimised: Weapon Mastery, Fast Attacks, Bullseye, Blast and Fireball-class spells, with typical armour for the level, and the Wounds and Death Rolls in the Core System. Their combined damage per round measured about 13 at Level 1, 19 at Level 3, 25 at Level 5 and 40 at Level 7 against the Generalist line. Group HP therefore runs about 2.4 to 3.3 rounds of their output.

Rescaling for your table. If your party deals noticeably more or less than the reference, scale every Group HP value by (your party’s damage per round) ÷ (reference damage per round) and recompute Solo HP. The HP cuts from v2.0 are the largest change in this Compendium; a casual or low-optimisation party may feel them as too light, in which case scale up rather than abandon the table.

Two things flagged in the Core System – one resolved

The first has been fixed in the Core. The second is still open and affects this Compendium directly.

1. Armour Rating as a block threshold – resolved. The Core System now has the Damage Floor and an Armour Rating cap of 8, which is the system-wide fix the v2.0 notes asked for. Rule 1 of this Compendium remains only as a pointer to it.

2. The instant-death clause. ‘If damage passed their HP maximum the player character dies regardless of how many death rolls they passed’ reads, in context, as applying to a character already at 0 Hit Points. It would be worth saying so explicitly. Under the wider reading – any single hit exceeding maximum Hit Points kills outright – a Level 7 Wind-Up at 34 damage would instantly kill most characters in the game from full Health with no death rolls at all, which is exactly the unavoidable death this pass is built to remove.

What was deliberately left alone

  • The ten archetype identities – Unchanged. They were well-shaped; the problem was the numbers underneath them, and the archetype identities are the best part of v1.2.

  • Combat Defence falling at Levels 6–7 – Big monsters staying easy to hit and hard to kill is correct, and it means low-Agility characters can still contribute against bosses.

  • The Warpriest’s low damage – Kept as-is. Cutting the healer’s damage to fund its healing is the right lever, and tying both the heal and the shield to Dmg/Hit keeps it to one number at the table.

  • The Blightcaller’s armour-ignoring damage – It was already the correct answer to the armour problem from the enemy side. The Damage Floor reduces how essential it is, which is healthy – the Blightcaller should be a good pick, not a mandatory one.

If it still is not lethal enough

Pull these in order, one at a time, testing between each:

  • Allow enemies of Level 4 or higher to Wind-Up every other round rather than treating it as occasional.

  • Raise Dmg/Hit by 2 across the board. Measured at Levels 3, 5 and 7 this adds roughly 0.2–0.5 Wounds per Standard fight. Do not raise HP – that lengthens fights without making them more dangerous.

  • Reduce the Damage Floor from half to a third of the attack’s damage. This is a house rule that overrides the Core System, so tell the table before you change it. It makes armour stronger and squishy characters relatively more exposed, so expect complaints from exactly one player.

  • Field escorts. Action economy is the most powerful lethality dial in the Compendium and the one that costs nothing to tune.

If it is too lethal

  • Announce Wind-Ups two rounds out instead of one.

  • Drop Dmg/Hit by 2 across the board before touching anything else.

  • Give the party a fourth Wound, or allow a full rest to heal all Wounds rather than two injuries.

  • Apply Morale at half Hit Points instead of a quarter.

Changelog v2.0 → v3.0

Everything below changed from v2.0. Anything not listed – archetype identities, trait wording not shown here, Morale, Breaking Off, the Wind-Up rule – is as before.

  • Atk bonus (new) – Flat per-Level attack bonus for enemies, +8 to +11. Specialists use the Generalist’s Atk.

  • Generalist Group HP – 40/56/72/92/116/144/180 became 32/36/48/56/68/104/132. Solo HP is Group ÷ 4.

  • Generalist Combat Defence – Level 2: 14 → 13. Level 4: 15 → 14. Cap is now 15 (was 16).

  • Generalist Dmg/Hit and Armour – Unchanged.

  • Specialist tables – All 20 rows re-priced. Group HP down 16–41%. Dmg/Hit: Skirmisher and Harrier L1 6 → 5, Blightcaller L4 6 → 4 and L7 11 → 6, Summoner L7 15 → 16. Combat Defence: Skirmisher and Harrier L4 16 → 15. Retaliator L5 Armour 5 → 4. Everything else is as before.

  • Warpriest – Mending Word heals ⅛ of the ally’s maximum HP instead of 4 × Dmg/Hit.

  • Retaliator – Spiked Bulwark is +1 (was +2).

  • Blightcaller – Virulent Strain is 4 damage for 3 turns (was 5); Level 7 base Dmg/Hit is 6.

  • Threat Budget – Rebuilt from simulation; added a Boss row and the Boss Action; removed the Level 8 Group row and the Warpriest/Summoner Deadly row.

  • Building More – Anti-Armour gains +15% Dmg/Hit; CD cap 15; Healer heal is ⅛ target max HP; Retaliation gains +1 Armour; Summoner HP −25%; Toxin Dmg/Hit −60%.

  • Rule 1 and Survival Levers – Damage Floor is a Core rule; Wounds now state the −1/−2/−3 rank cost.

Generalist before and after:

Simulation notes

Each fight is resolved round by round with the Core System’s attack roll, criticals, Damage Floor, Armour cap, three Wounds and Death Rolls. Party members attack the enemy with the lowest Hit Points, preferring healers and summoners, and spend a Wound when a hit would drop them to 0. Enemies spread their attacks across the party, and Level 4+ enemies announce and fire their Wind-Up once per fight unless it is interrupted.

Specialists were checked by replacing one generalist with the specialist in a Standard fight and comparing Wounds and rounds to the all-generalist baseline. Every specialist landed within roughly ±25% of the baseline on Wounds, with the swing in either direction that the ‘fielded against its counter’ note in the Specialist section describes.

This is a model of a reasonable party, not of every table. Treat the numbers as a calibrated starting point and move one row up or down the ladder when your table’s experience says so.
